## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

- **The whole application is the unit.** §3's four properties (composition,
  locality, continuity, performance) are the right acceptance vocabulary.
  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
counterexamples never become reachability proofs. The exposure map includes
inherited grants and their writers; new advice covers role/grant lifetimes,
grant arguments/global bootstrap, omitted evidence, sweep timing and refusal
oracles. The guide and all shipped todo/social/chat/production starter schemas
state their privacy intent. One new online-only guide mutation updates the
authoring census; the 94 private eligible sites stay unchanged.

Reports and all 150 schema hashes: `reports/20261018-sec/`. Main 20k and device
0–999 differentials have no disagreements; Lean's four known signature classes
retain the exact integration15 counts. Whole-protocol/device freshness,
general aggregate/time reductions, actual unauthenticated/revoked-device
campaigns and the repaired 015 custody workflow remain explicit gaps in
`../issues/20261018-sb4-security-proof-and-adversarial-qualification-gaps.md`.
This is a conservative verification foundation, not a zero-holes capability
claim. No version bump, push or publication.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG staging clock control

Classified staging stores admit serve --clock-offset and the existing clock
advance/status wire under a full service token. Production refuses offsets
before listener startup and all HTTP clock control even with operator authority.
The writer rechecks classification/session before advancing. startServe exposes
clockOffset, now and advance; it mints a current staging-clock operator session
for each control rather than caching a token across arbitrarily large jumps.
Fixture/reset/persona/job dev doors remain closed. The native-backed client
regression passed, including CLI advancement and restart dropping the offset.
The installed-package staging qualifier now exercises the same clock helpers.
LLP 3000.000.000.002 records the combined MIG language/adoption amendment;
independent review continues before final acceptance.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG declarative backfills and device bytes

Backfills are typed scalar patches evaluated before candidate narrowing, with
per-lifetime preimages and declaration membership retained through dormancy.
The existing activation transaction stages every result, checks historical
lookup uniqueness, then publishes data/indexes/derived state and generation.
Whole-table and narrowed restoration join the source snapshot. Hot adoption
and exact previews sample the serving clock, including staging offsets.

The web schema omits server-only migration metadata and evidence. Locked Linux
measurement is 2,385,285 B raw / 726,681 B gzip, +4,796 raw / +679 gzip versus
Integration 17. The small deterministic text library must use the same Rust
interpreter on server and device; the artifact allowance moves to measured
727,181 B (exactly 500 B headroom). No runtime meter ceiling changes. Family
and absent-feature audits and final qualification are recorded in the MIG
report; this measurement alone does not claim those checks have passed.

2026-10-19 MIG final wasm: metered text argument accounting measures 2,385,447 raw / 726,623 gzip bytes; budget tightened to 727,123 (measurement +500). Server-only backfill metadata remains absent from web wasm.

   270	fn validate_lookup(tx:&Tx<'_>,ti:i64,prefix:usize,table:&str,index:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   271	    let mut stmt=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT k,id FROM i WHERE ti=?1 ORDER BY k")?;let mut rows=stmt.query([ti])?;
   272	    let mut prior=None;let mut first=String::new();let mut duplicate=false;let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();

   274	        let key:Vec<u8>=row.get(0)?;let id:String=row.get(1)?;let mut group=crate::key::decode(&key).ok_or_else(||refusal("invalid historical lookup key"))?;group.truncate(prefix);
   275	        if group.contains(&Value::Null){continue;}
   276	        if prior.as_ref()==Some(&group) {
   277	            if !duplicate {bad.add(&first,"duplicate historical lookup key");duplicate=true;}
   278	            bad.add(&id,"duplicate historical lookup key");
   279	        }else{prior=Some(group);first=id;duplicate=false;}
   280	    }
   281	    if bad.count>0{return Err(refusal(format!("historical lookup {table}.{index} is not unique: {}; prepare a unique bridge before deriving values",bad.describe())))}
   282	    Ok(())
   283	}

   285	// Each original adoption cohort saw one snapshot. Re-entry restores that same
   286	// view across all its declarations; later cohorts read earlier cohorts' outputs.
   287	fn restore_snapshot(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   288	    tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=(SELECT o.data FROM temp._backfill_originals o WHERE o.t=r.t AND o.id=r.id) WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM temp._backfill_originals o WHERE o.t=r.t AND o.id=r.id)",[])?;Ok(())
   289	}
   290	fn historical_snapshot(tx:&Tx<'_>,epoch:i64,keys:&BTreeSet<String>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   291	    let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT t,id,definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=1 AND epoch=?1 ORDER BY t,id,definition")?;
   292	    let mut rows=q.query([epoch])?;
   293	    while let Some(r)=rows.next()? {
   294	        let t:i64=r.get(0)?;let id:String=r.get(1)?;let key:String=r.get(2)?;if !keys.contains(&key){continue;}
   295	        let data:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   296	        let Some(data)=data else {continue};
   297	        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._backfill_originals SELECT t,id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
   298	        let mut row:Value=decode(&data)?;let e=evidence(tx,t,&id,&key)?.unwrap();
   299	        let fields=match &mut row {Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=>f,_=>return Err(refusal("invalid historical row"))};
   300	        if let Value::Row(inputs)|Value::Map(inputs)=e.inputs {for(c,value)in inputs {
   301	            if e.absent.contains(&c) || !fields.contains_key(&c) || fields.get(&c)==Some(&value) {
   302	                let invalid:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND invalid=1)",params![t,id,input_ref(&key,&c)],|r|r.get(0))?;
   303	                if invalid {return Err(refusal(format!("historical backfill reference {c} on {id} lost its parent lifetime")));}
   304	                if e.absent.contains(&c) {fields.insert(c,value);}else{fields.entry(c).or_insert(value);}
   305	            }
   306	        }}
   307	        for c in e.after.keys() {if !e.before.contains_key(c) {fields.insert(c.clone(),Value::Null);}}
   308	        for(c,value)in e.before {fields.insert(c,value);}
   309	        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;
   310	    } Ok(())
   311	}
   312	fn rebuild_reads(tx:&Tx<'_>,source:&Schema,reads:&BTreeSet<(String,String)>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   313	    for (name,index) in reads {
   314	        let old=&source.tables[name].indexes[index];let ti=tx.num(&format!("{name}.{index}"))?;
   315	        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti=?1",[ti])?;
   316	        super::activation::walk(tx,tx.num(name)?,|id,mut row|{
   317	            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,&source.tables[name]);
   318	            for key in index_keys(old,&row,id){tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO i(ti,k,id) VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![ti,key,id])?;}Ok(())
   319	        })?;
   320	        if old.unique {validate_lookup(tx,ti,old.components.len(),name,index)?;}
   321	    } Ok(())
   322	}

   324	fn input_ref(key:&str,column:&str)->String {format!("$backfill:{key}:{column}")}

   326	// A prior transformation's retained added output is not its own old argument
   327	// on forward selection after rollback. Other declarations still see the new
   328	// predecessor snapshot rather than an old cohort's overwritten columns.
   329	fn inactive_snapshot(tx:&Tx<'_>,previous:Option<&Schema>,keys:&BTreeSet<String>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   330	    let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT t,id,definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=0 ORDER BY t,id,definition")?;
   331	    let mut rows=q.query([])?;
   332	    while let Some(r)=rows.next()? {
   333	        let t:i64=r.get(0)?;let id:String=r.get(1)?;let key:String=r.get(2)?;if !keys.contains(&key){continue;}
   334	        let name:String=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT name FROM _catalog WHERE num=?1",[t],|r|r.get(0))?;
   335	        let old=previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&name));
   336	        let e=evidence(tx,t,&id,&key)?.unwrap();
   337	        let absent:Vec<_>=e.after.keys().filter(|c|!e.before.contains_key(*c) && old.is_none_or(|t|!t.columns.contains_key(*c))).cloned().collect();
   338	        if absent.is_empty(){continue;}
   339	        let data:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;let Some(data)=data else{continue};
   340	        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._backfill_originals SELECT t,id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
   341	        let mut row:Value=decode(&data)?;if let Value::Row(fields)|Value::Map(fields)=&mut row {for c in absent {fields.insert(c,Value::Null);}}
   342	        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;
   343	    }Ok(())
   344	}

   346	// Empty definition denotes currently omitted physical input cells; a definition
   347	// digest denotes absence at that receipt's adoption. Defaults may materialize
   348	// during retention without turning omitted inputs into authored values.
   349	pub(crate) fn prepare_inputs(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,previous:Option<&Schema>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   350	    if next.backfills.is_empty() && previous.is_none_or(|s|s.backfills.is_empty()) && !present(tx)? {return Ok(());}
   351	    enable(tx)?;
   352	    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_active",[])?;
   353	    for fill in &next.backfills {tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _backfill_active VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![tx.num(&fill.table)?,fill.key(),canonical_json(&fill.inputs.keys().filter(|c|!fill.outputs.contains(*c)).collect::<Vec<_>>())])?;}
   354	    for fill in next.backfills.iter().chain(previous.into_iter().flat_map(|s|s.backfills.iter())) {
   355	        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_definitions VALUES(?1,?2)",params![fill.key(),canonical_json(fill)])?;
   356	        for c in fill.inputs.keys() {tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_inputs VALUES(?1,?2)",params![tx.num(&fill.table)?,c])?;}
   357	    }
   358	    let tables:Vec<i64>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT t FROM _backfill_inputs")?.query_map([],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
   359	    for t in tables {
   360	        let columns:Vec<String>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT column_name FROM _backfill_inputs WHERE t=?1")?.query_map([t],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
   361	        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,row|{
   362	            for c in &columns {if row.field(c).is_err(){note_omission(tx,t,id,c)?;}}
   363	            Ok(())
   364	        })?;
   365	    } Ok(())
   366	}
   367	pub(super) fn observe_write(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,table:&Table,row:&Value,old:Option<&Value>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   368	    if !present(tx)? {return Ok(());}
   369	    if old.is_none() {
   370	        let active:Vec<(String,String)>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT definition,input_names FROM _backfill_active WHERE t=?1")?.query_map([t],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
   371	        for (key,names) in active {
   372	            let names:Vec<String>=decode(&names)?;let absent=names.iter().filter(|c|row.field(c).is_err()).cloned().collect();
   373	            let fingerprint=input_fingerprint(row,names.iter().map(String::as_str),&absent);
   374	            tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_absence VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4)",params![t,id,format!("$native:{key}"),fingerprint])?;
   375	        }
   376	    }
   377	    let columns:Vec<String>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT column_name FROM _backfill_inputs WHERE t=?1")?.query_map([t],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
   378	    if columns.is_empty(){return Ok(());}
   379	    let interpreted_old=old.map(|r|crate::schema::row_image(r,table));
   380	    let interpreted_new=crate::schema::row_image(row,table);
   381	    for c in columns {
   382	        let changed=interpreted_old.as_ref().is_none_or(|before|before.field(&c).ok()!=interpreted_new.field(&c).ok());
   383	        if changed {tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_absence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND definition NOT LIKE '$native:%'",params![t,id,c])?;}
   384	        if row.field(&c).is_err() {note_omission(tx,t,id,&c)?;}
   385	    } Ok(())
   386	}
   387	
   388	pub(super) fn record_omissions(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,row:&Value)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   389	    if !present(tx)? {return Ok(());}
   390	    let columns:Vec<String>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT column_name FROM _backfill_inputs WHERE t=?1")?.query_map([t],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
   391	    for c in columns {if row.field(&c).is_err(){note_omission(tx,t,id,&c)?;}}Ok(())
   392	}
   393	
   394	fn input_fingerprint<'a>(row:&Value,names:impl Iterator<Item=&'a str>,absent:&BTreeSet<String>)->String {
   395	    let fields:BTreeMap<String,Value>=names.map(|c|(c.to_owned(),if absent.contains(c){Value::Null}else{row.field(c).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null)})).collect();
   396	    crate::value::digest(&fields)
   397	}
   398	
   399	fn note_omission(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,c:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   400	    // A provided historical cell stays provided when merely moved out of the
   401	    // active projection. Already-recorded omission survives default freezing.
   402	    tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_absence SELECT ?1,?2,'',?3 WHERE NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3)",params![t,id,c])?;Ok(())
   403	}
   404	fn native_inputs<'a>(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,row:&Value,names:impl Iterator<Item=&'a str>,absent:&BTreeSet<String>)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Value>,RuntimeError>{
   405	    let mut out=BTreeMap::new();let mut meter=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);
   406	    for c in names {
   407	        let value=if absent.contains(c){Value::Null}else if let Ok(value)=row.field(c){meter.heap(value.retained_heap_bytes().saturating_mul(64).saturating_add(8192))?;value.clone()}else{
   408	            let bytes:Option<i64>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT octet_length(data) FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",params![t,id,c],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   409	            meter.heap(bytes.unwrap_or(0).max(0) as u64 * 64 + 8192)?;
   410	            let text:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",params![t,id,c],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   411	            text.map(|s|decode(&s)).transpose()?.unwrap_or(Value::Null)
   412	        };out.insert(c.to_owned(),value);
   413	    }Ok(out)
   414	}
   415	
   416	// @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — source expiry is an ordinary reference-action closure, never an unchecked physical delete.
   417	pub(super) fn retire_snapshot(tx:&Tx<'_>,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,previous:Option<&Schema>,now:i64)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   418	    let mut source=previous.cloned().unwrap_or_default();source.tables=sources.clone();source.backfills.clear();source.prepare_receipt_dependencies();
   419	    let names:Vec<String>=source.through_order().into_iter().rev().filter(|n|source.tables[n].ephemeral.is_none() && source.maintain_for(n).is_none()).collect();
   420	    let mut due=false;
   421	    for name in &names {
   422	        let t=tx.num(name)?;
   423	        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,row|{if !due {due=source_expired(tx,t,id,&source.tables[name],&row,now)?;}Ok(())})?;
   424	    }
   425	    if !due{return Ok(())}
   426	    // Restored sources have no live index entries. Every reference action,
   427	    // maintained dependency and retained-child probe uses this assembled view.
   428	    for name in &names {
   429	        let table=&source.tables[name];
   430	        for (index,definition) in &table.indexes {
   431	            let ti=tx.num(&format!("{name}.{index}"))?;tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti=?1",[ti])?;
   432	            let work=crate::rows::adoption::Work::table(table);
   433	            let scratch=|row:&Value|crate::rows::adoption::contribution_bytes(row,table);
   434	            super::activation_walk::walk_metered(tx,tx.num(name)?,work,if work.index_entries!=0 {Some(&scratch)}else{None},|id,mut row,_|{
   435	                materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);
   436	                for key in index_keys(definition,&row,id){tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO i(ti,k,id) VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![ti,key,id])?;}Ok(())
   437	            })?;
   438	        }
   439	    }
   440	    if tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_cap_counts')",[],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
   441	        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _cap_counts",[])?;
   442	        for name in &names {let table=&source.tables[name];super::activation::walk(tx,tx.num(name)?,|_,row|tx.update_cap_counts(name,table,None,Some(&row)).map(|_|()))?;}
   443	    }
   444	    validate_action_bounds(tx,&source,now)?;
   445	    tx.clear_activation_scan();
   446	    for name in &names {
   447	        let t=tx.num(name)?;
   448	        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,_|{
   449	            let Some(mut row)=tx.get(name,id)? else{return Ok(())};
   450	            if !source_expired(tx,t,id,&source.tables[name],&row,now)? {return Ok(())}
   451	            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,&source.tables[name]);
   452	            let mut runner=Runner::new(tx,&source,Context::system().at(now),Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort),tx.next_seq()?);
   453	            match runner.retire_expired_row(name,&row) {
   454	                Ok(true)=>{tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;},
   455	                Ok(false)=>return Err(refusal(format!("source expiry {name}/{id} is blocked by retained children; resolve them in a bridge before adoption"))),
   456	                Err(e)=>return Err(refusal(format!("source expiry {name}/{id}: {e}"))),
   457	            }Ok(())
   458	        })?;
   459	    }
   460	    for name in &names {
   461	        let cleared:Vec<_>=source.tables[name].on_delete.iter().filter(|(_,a)|**a==crate::schema::references::OnDelete::SetNull).map(|(c,_)|c.clone()).collect();
   462	        if cleared.is_empty(){continue;}
   463	        super::activation::walk(tx,tx.num(name)?,|id,row|{
   464	            for c in &cleared {if row.field(c).is_ok_and(|v|*v==Value::Null){tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",params![tx.num(name)?,id,c])?;}}Ok(())
   465	        })?;
   466	    }
   467	    tx.clear_activation_scan();Ok(())
   468	}
   469	fn source_expired(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,table:&Table,row:&Value,now:i64)->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
   470	    if table.expired(row,now){return Ok(true)}
   471	    let definition:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT definition FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   472	    if let Some(definition)=definition {let old:Table=decode(&definition)?;return Ok(old.expired(row,now));}Ok(false)
   473	}
   474	
   475	fn validate_action_bounds(tx:&Tx<'_>,source:&Schema,now:i64)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   476	    for parent in source.tables.keys() {for r in source.referencing(parent).into_iter().filter(|r|r.action.writes()) {
   477	        let bound=source.reference_cap(r.child,r.column).ok_or_else(||refusal("historical reference action has no bound"))?;
   478	        let index=source.reference_index(r.child,r.column).ok_or_else(||refusal("historical reference index absent"))?;
   479	        let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT k FROM i WHERE ti=?1 ORDER BY k")?;let mut rows=q.query([tx.num(&format!("{}.{index}",r.child))?])?;
   480	        let mut prior=None;let mut count=0u64;let mut visited=0u64;let mut meter=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);
   481	        while let Some(row)=rows.next()? {
   482	            if visited%256==0 {meter=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);}visited+=1;
   483	            let mark=meter.mark();let key=row.get_ref(0)?.as_blob().map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;meter.heap(key.len() as u64 * 64 + 8192)?;
   484	            let values=crate::key::decode(key).ok_or_else(||refusal("invalid historical reference key"))?;
   485	            if values.first().is_none_or(|v|*v==Value::Null) || r.table.is_expiring() && values.get(1).is_some_and(|v|matches!(v,Value::Int(at) if *at<=now)) {meter.release_heap(mark);continue;}
   486	            if prior.as_ref()==values.first(){count+=1;}else{prior=values.first().cloned();count=1;}
   487	            if count>u64::from(bound){return Err(refusal(format!("historical reference group {}.{} exceeds retirement bound {bound}; reconcile retained capacity in a bridge before adoption",r.child,r.column)));}
   488	            meter.release_heap(mark);
   489	        }
   490	    }}Ok(())
   491	}

//! Historical row transforms, separate from application-callable programs.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#2-language — the compiler and raw-schema boundary share a closed expression fragment.
use super::*;
use crate::ir::{Builtin, Expr, Program, ProgramKind, Stmt};
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Backfill {
    pub table: String,
    pub name: String,
    pub inputs: BTreeMap<String, ColumnType>,
    pub outputs: BTreeSet<String>,
    pub program: Program,
}
impl Backfill {
    pub fn key(&self) -> String { digest(&self.normalized()) }
    pub fn normalized(&self) -> Self {
        // Locations are diagnostic metadata, never transformation identity.
        let mut normalized = self.clone();
        fn expr(e: &mut Expr) {
            match e {
                Expr::Get(r) | Expr::Exists(r) => { r.site.clear(); for e in &mut r.key { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Scan(r) => {
                    r.site.clear();
                    for k in &mut r.prefix { if let crate::ir::IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(e)=k {expr(e);} }
                    if let Some(e)=&mut r.filter {expr(e);}
                    if let Some(r)=&mut r.range {for e in [&mut r.gt,&mut r.gte,&mut r.lt,&mut r.lte].into_iter().flatten(){expr(e);}}
                }
                Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => expr(base),
                Expr::Array(es) | Expr::Builtin { args: es, .. } => { for e in es { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Object(fs) => { for e in fs.values_mut() { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Shape { base, fields } => { expr(base); for e in fields.values_mut() { expr(e); } }
                Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { expr(left); expr(right); }
                Expr::Block { body, value } => { statements(body); expr(value); }
                _ => (),
            }
        }
        fn statements(body: &mut [Stmt]) {
            for s in body {
                match s {
                    Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr(value),
                    Stmt::Require { condition, site, .. } => { *site=None; expr(condition); },
                    Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => { expr(condition); statements(then_body); statements(else_body); }
                    _ => (),
                }
            }
        }
        statements(&mut normalized.program.body);
        normalized
    }
    pub fn admit(&self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), String> {
        let table = schema.table(&self.table).ok_or("backfill names an absent table")?;
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() || schema.maintain_for(&self.table).is_some() { return Err("backfill requires an authoritative durable table".into()); }
        if self.program.kind != ProgramKind::Query || self.program.native || self.outputs.is_empty() { return Err("backfill must produce a nonempty object patch".into()); }
        for c in &self.outputs {
            let kind = table.columns.get(c).ok_or_else(||format!("backfill output {}.{c} is not declared",self.table))?;
            if c=="id" || table.engine_field(c) || table.maintained.contains_key(c) || table.expire.as_ref()==Some(c) || table.not_before.as_ref()==Some(c) || !scalar(kind) {
                return Err(format!("backfill output {}.{c} must be an authoritative scalar, not an asset, lifetime or captured field",self.table));
            }
        }
        if !body(&self.program.body,schema) { return Err("backfill expressions allow deterministic scalar transforms, unique point lookups and bounded existence; no viewer, clock, ids, row collections, loops or writes".into()); }
        Ok(())
    }
}
fn scalar(k: &ColumnType) -> bool {
    match k {
        ColumnType::Optional(k) => scalar(k),
        ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. } | ColumnType::List { .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { .. } => false,
        _ => true,
    }
}
fn body(stmts: &[Stmt], s: &Schema) -> bool {
    stmts.iter().all(|stmt| match stmt {
        Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr(value,s),
        Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr(condition,s),
        Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => expr(condition,s) && body(then_body,s) && body(else_body,s),
        _ => false,
    })
}
fn expr(e: &Expr, s: &Schema) -> bool {
    match e {
        Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Variable(_) => true,
        Expr::Input(_) | Expr::Merge(_) => false,
        Expr::Scan(r) => r.existence && r.after.is_none() && r.before.is_none() && r.prefix.iter().all(|k| k.scalar().is_some_and(|e|expr(e,s))) && r.filter.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|expr(e,s)) && r.range.as_ref().is_none_or(|r|[&r.gt,&r.gte,&r.lt,&r.lte].iter().all(|e|e.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|expr(e,s)))),
        Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => expr(base,s),
        Expr::Array(es) => es.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Object(es) => es.values().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Shape { base, fields } => expr(base,s) && fields.values().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => expr(left,s) && expr(right,s),
        Expr::Block { body: stmts, value } => body(stmts,s) && expr(value,s),
        Expr::Get(r) | Expr::Exists(r) => s.table(&r.table).is_some_and(|t| t.index(&r.index).is_some_and(|i|i.unique && i.is_plain() && i.components.len()==r.key.len())) && r.key.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
        Expr::Builtin { builtin, args } => matches!(builtin,
            Builtin::RequireUncapped | Builtin::ProjectFields { .. } | Builtin::OptionalField { .. } | Builtin::Len | Builtin::Count | Builtin::Slice | Builtin::Concat | Builtin::Lower | Builtin::Trim | Builtin::Text(_) | Builtin::Sha256 | Builtin::First | Builtin::Last | Builtin::At | Builtin::Min | Builtin::Max | Builtin::Divide { .. } | Builtin::Civil(_) | Builtin::Calendar { .. }
        ) && args.len()==builtin.arity() && args.iter().all(|e|expr(e,s)),
    }
}
//! Bind historical arguments without requiring them in the current row type.
use super::*;
use crate::{ast, diag::{type_error, unknown}, types::{Ty, scalar_to_column}};
use snapback4_core::schema::backfill::Backfill;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
pub(super) fn bind(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &mut Schema, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut names = BTreeSet::new(); let mut outputs = BTreeSet::new();
    for d in catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d| &d.backfills) {
        if !names.insert((d.table.clone(),d.name.clone())) { diagnostics.push(unknown(&d.site,"duplicate backfill name on this table","give each historical transform its own name")); continue; }
        let decl = ast::ProgramDecl { delivery: None, held:false, predicts:false, server:false, kind:ast::ProgramKind::Query,
            name: format!("backfill{}{}",d.table,d.name), args:d.args.clone(), prelude:vec![],
            body:vec![ast::Stmt::Return { value:d.value.clone(), site:d.site.clone() }], native:false, site:d.site.clone() };
        let Some(bound) = program::Binder::bind(catalog,schema,"schema",&decl,diagnostics) else {continue};
        let Ty::Object(fields) = &bound.result else { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,"backfill returns an object patch","{ column: historicalValue }")); continue; };
        let Some(table) = schema.table(&d.table) else {diagnostics.push(unknown(&d.site,"backfill table is not declared","declare the candidate table"));continue};
        for (c,ty) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = table.columns.get(c) {
                if !ty.assignable_to(&Ty::from_column(kind)) { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,format!("backfill {}.{c} returns {}, expected {}",d.table,ty.name(),kind),"return a value fitting the candidate column")); }
            }
            if !outputs.insert((d.table.clone(),c.clone())) { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,format!("backfills overlap on {}.{c}",d.table),"combine simultaneous outputs in one backfill")); }
        }
        let fill = Backfill { table:d.table.clone(), name:d.name.clone(), inputs:d.args.iter().map(|a|(a.name.clone(),scalar_to_column(&a.type_))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>(), outputs:fields.keys().cloned().collect(), program:bound.program };
        if let Err(why) = fill.admit(schema) { diagnostics.push(type_error(&d.site,why,"use deterministic historical fields and a scalar patch").guide("deploy")); }
        schema.backfills.push(fill.normalized());
    }
}

//! Candidate activation: project rows, validate invariants, build indexes
//! and derived rows, then publish the semantic generation in one commit.
// @ref LLP 3000.000#implementation-follow-up — one atomic activation boundary

pub(super) use super::activation_walk::walk;
use super::activation_walk::walk_metered;
use super::*;
use crate::ir::Program;
use crate::schema::{Constraint, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::value::digest;
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

pub(super) type Identity = (Option<String>, Option<String>, Option<String>, Option<String>,
);

pub(super) fn identity(conn: &Connection) -> Result<Identity, RuntimeError> {
    let mut statement = conn.prepare_cached("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1")?;
    let mut get = |key| statement.query_row([key], |row| row.get(0)).optional();
    Ok((get("schema_hash")?, get("activation_hash")?, get("generation")?, get("device:generation")?,
    ))
}

impl Store {
    /// Install a device's schema. Server engines also supply their programs
    /// and derived-state builder through `activate` below.
    pub fn install(&mut self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.activate(schema, None, false, |_, _| Ok(())).map(|_| ())
    }

    /// A device can discard an obsolete partition while atomically caching
    /// its replacement backend and reset watermark. It never migrates rows
    /// whose authority belongs to a previous server generation.
    pub fn install_device(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.install_device_checked(schema, metadata, reset, &BTreeMap::new())
    }

    pub fn install_device_checked(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.install_device_checked_with(schema, metadata, reset, expected, |_| Ok(()))
    }

    /// Device-owned sidecar changes share the schema installation transaction.
    pub fn install_device_checked_with(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>, prepare: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.install_device_checked_with_finish(schema, metadata, reset, expected, prepare, |_| {
            Ok(())
        })
    }

    /// Opening recovery and its final reads must finish before installation commits.
    pub fn install_device_checked_with_finish<R>(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>, prepare: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, finish: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<R, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<R, RuntimeError> {
        let mut result = None;
        self.activate(schema, None, reset, |tx, _| {
            for (key, value) in expected {
                let held: Option<String> = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1", [key], |row| {
                        row.get(0)
                    }).optional()?;
                if &held != value { return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the backend adoption capture is obsolete; retry sync".into())); }
            }
            prepare(tx)?;
            for (key, value) in metadata {
                // A device metadata namespace reset shares backend installation's transaction.
                if key.ends_with(':') {
                    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key >= ?1 AND key < ?2", params![key, format!("{key}\u{10ffff}")])?;
                } else { tx.set_meta(key, value)?; }
            }
            if reset { tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _device_base; DELETE FROM _device_snapshot;")?; }
            result = Some(finish(tx)?);
            Ok(())
        })?;
        Ok(result.expect("activation ran opening recovery"))
    }

    pub(crate) fn activate(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.activate_at(schema, programs, reset, crate::now_ms(), |tx, changed, _| build(tx, changed))
    }

    pub(crate) fn activate_at(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        // One-shot device preparation is never retried or given a larger cache.
        self.activate_transaction(schema, programs, reset, now, true, build)
    }

    pub(crate) fn activate_at_with_launch<R>(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, allow_launch: bool, check: impl Fn(&Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, mut build: impl FnMut(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, finish: impl Fn(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<R, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<(u64, R), RuntimeError> {
        let budget = if programs.is_some() { super::activation_cache::configured_mib()? } else { 0 };
        // The server builder owns only transaction-local work. Recreate all of
        // it on retry, with the same schema/programs/activation time.
        self.with_activation_cache(budget, |store| {
            store.activate_transaction_with(schema, programs, reset, now, allow_launch, &check, &mut build, &finish)
        })
    }

    pub(super) fn activate_transaction(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, allow_launch: bool, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.activate_transaction_with(schema, programs, reset, now, allow_launch, |_| Ok(()), build, |_| Ok(())).map(|(generation, ())| generation)
    }

    fn activate_transaction_with<R>(&mut self, schema: &Schema, programs: Option<&BTreeMap<String, Program>>, reset: bool, now: i64, allow_launch: bool, check: impl FnOnce(&Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, build: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>, bool, &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, finish: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<R, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<(u64, R), RuntimeError> {

                "SELECT c.name, COUNT(*) FROM i JOIN _catalog c ON c.num = i.ti WHERE length(i.k) > ?1 GROUP BY i.ti ORDER BY i.ti LIMIT 1",
                [crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES as i64], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?))
            ).optional()?;

            let restore=tx.meta("activation_restore")?.as_deref()!=Some("false");
            retained_through=super::dormant::transition(&tx,schema,previous.as_ref(),restore,now,&mut report)?;
        }
        let population_changed=tx.meta("activation_population")?.as_deref()==Some("true");
        changed |= population_changed;
        let rebuilding=schema_changed || population_changed;

                    serde_json::from_str(&s).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::decode(e,RuntimeError::Store))
                }).transpose()?;
            let shortened = crate::engine::through_activation::activate_retained(&tx,schema,previous.as_ref(),now,&retained_through)?;
            tx.set_meta("read_through_shortened",&canonical_json(&shortened))?;
            report.detail("read-through retirement", started);
        }
        if programs.is_some() { crate::engine::timeline::check_activation(&tx, schema)?; }
        if rebuilding && programs.is_some() {
            let started = std::time::Instant::now();
            super::timeline_launch::build(&tx, schema, &launches, now)?;
            report.detail("feed launch", started);
            let started = std::time::Instant::now();
            super::event_slots::fill(&tx, schema, now)?;
            report.detail("event slot fill", started);
            let started = std::time::Instant::now();
            if super::dormant::enabled(&tx)? {
                let old:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')",[],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
                let old:Option<Schema>=old.map(|s| {
                        serde_json::from_str(&s).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))
                    }).transpose()?;
                super::adopt_refusals::candidate(&tx,schema,if tx.meta("activation_population")?.as_deref()==Some("true"){None}else{old.as_ref()},false)?;
            }
            if let Some(step) = super::successor::assess(&tx, schema)?.iter().find(|s| s.change == crate::successor::Change::Narrowing) {
                return Err(step.refusal(schema));
            }
            report.detail("successor assessment", started);
        }
        let previous: Option<Schema> = if rebuilding && programs.is_some() && !reset {
            tx.conn().query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation = (SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key = 'generation')", [], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0)).optional()?
                .map(|s| serde_json::from_str(&s).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::decode(e, RuntimeError::Store))).transpose()?
        } else { None };
        #[cfg(test)]
        let previous = if self.full_adoption { None } else { previous };
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#13-wave-13-preview-decisions — report interpreted literal fills from the committed candidate population.
        if let Some(previous) = &previous {
            for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
                let fills: Vec<_> = table.defaults.iter().filter_map(|(column, default)| {
                    let same_interpretation=previous.table(name).is_some_and(|old|
                        old.defaults.get(column)==Some(default) && old.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind|!kind.is_optional())
                        && !old.maintained.contains_key(column) && !old.engine_field(column));
                    if same_interpretation { return None; }
                    if table.columns[column].is_optional() || table.maintained.contains_key(column) || table.engine_field(column) { return None; }
                    match default { crate::schema::ColumnDefault::Literal(value) => Some((column, value)), _ => None }
                }).collect();
                if fills.is_empty() || schema.maintain_for(name).is_some() || table.ephemeral.is_some() { continue; }
                let mut counts = vec![0u64; fills.len()];
                walk(&tx, catalog[name], |_, row| {
                    for (i,(column,_)) in fills.iter().enumerate() { if row.field(column).is_err() { counts[i] += 1; } }
                    Ok(())
                })?;
                for ((column,value),count) in fills.into_iter().zip(counts).filter(|(_,count)| *count > 0) {
                    let value = match value { Value::String(text) => format!("'{}'", text.replace('\\', "\\\\").replace('\'', "\\'").replace('\n', "\\n").replace('\r', "\\r")), value => crate::wire::to_json(value).to_string() };
                    report.backfills.push(format!("{name}.{column} ← {value} on {count} {}",if count==1 {"row"} else {"rows"}));
                }
            }
        }
        let plans = super::activation_plan::plans(schema, if population_changed {None} else {previous.as_ref()});
        if let Some(previous)=&previous {
            for step in crate::successor::classify(previous,schema) {
                let parts=step.path.split('.').collect::<Vec<_>>();
                let label=if parts.first()==Some(&"tables") && parts.len()>=2 {
                    let name=parts[1];
                    let label=super::adoption_report::table_label(if schema.table(name).is_some(){schema}else{previous},name);
                    if label==name && parts.get(2)==Some(&"columns") && parts.get(3).is_some_and(|c|!c.starts_with('$')) {format!("{label}.{}",parts[3])}else{label}
                } else if let Some(position)=step.path.strip_prefix("maintains[").and_then(|s|s.strip_suffix(']')).and_then(|s|s.parse::<usize>().ok()) {
                    schema.maintains.get(position).map_or_else(||"view".into(),|m|m.label())
                } else {"backend".into()};
                let message=format!("{label} changed");
                if !report.changes.contains(&message){report.changes.push(message);}
            }
        }

        report.phase("preflight", &mut phase);
        if rebuilding {
            // @ref LLP 3000.000#implementation-follow-up — keys are still evaluated,
            // admitted and published in this transaction. Spill the key stream to
            // SQLite's temporary store, then insert in B-tree order instead of
            // repeatedly dirtying scattered main-database pages for every row.
            let ordered = programs.is_some() && plans.values().any(|plan| !plan.indexes.is_empty());
            #[cfg(test)]
            let ordered = ordered && !self.full_adoption;
            if ordered {
                super::activation_index::create(&tx)?;
            }
            // Removed names retain only their catalog number, never live row
            // images. Reintroducing a name starts empty, without resurrection.
            let active = super::activation_plan::active_names(schema);
            for (name, num) in &catalog {
                if !active.contains(name) {
                    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM r WHERE t = ?1", [num])?;
                    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti = ?1", [num])?;
                }
            }
            for (name, table) in schema.tables.iter().filter(|(_,t)| t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
                let t = catalog[name];
                let plan = &plans[name];
                // Launch put() already built these fresh indexes under the
                // complete candidate schema. Keep the final invariant pass,
                // but do not delete and reconstruct the same entries again.
                if launches.iter().any(|launch| {
                    schema.timelines.iter().any(|timeline| {
                        timeline.name == launch.name && ["Posts", "Work", "Edges", "Feed"].iter()
                        .any(|suffix| timeline.table(suffix) == *name)
                    })
                }) { continue; }
                if !plan.project && plan.indexes.is_empty() { continue; }
                let started = std::time::Instant::now();
                for index in &plan.indexes { tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti = ?1", [catalog[&format!("{name}.{index}")]])?; }
                let work = crate::rows::adoption::Work::table(table);
                let scratch = |row: &Value| crate::rows::adoption::contribution_bytes(row, table);
                walk_metered(&tx, t, work, if work.index_entries != 0 { Some(&scratch) } else { None }, |id, mut row, contribution_meter| {
                    let keep = |column: &str| {
                            table.columns.contains_key(column) || (programs.is_none() && column == "pending")
                        };
                    let project = plan.project && row.as_object().is_ok_and(|fields| fields.keys().any(|column| !keep(column)));
                    // Persist only a destructive projection. Defaults and
                    // optionals are an interpretation of the stored image.
                    if project {
                        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row { fields.retain(|column, _| keep(column)); }
                        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data = ?1 WHERE t = ?2 AND id = ?3", params![canonical_json(&row), t, id])?;
                    }
                    materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                    // A device's prediction mark is metadata, retained in
                    // storage but absent from the row the schema validates.
                    if programs.is_none() { if let Value::Row(fields) = &mut row { fields.remove("pending"); } }
                    // Maintained rows are replaced by the candidate builder.
                    if schema.maintain_for(name).is_none() { crate::engine::validate::validate_row(schema, name, &row)?; }
                    for index_name in &plan.indexes {
                        let index = &table.indexes[index_name];
                        let scratch = contribution_meter.mark();
                        let contribution = match index.components.first() { Some(crate::schema::IndexComponent::Contribution(m)) => Some(m), _ => None };
                        if let Some(m) = contribution {
                            contribution_meter.at(&format!("adopt {name}.{index_name}"));
                            contribution_meter.charge(crate::error::MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
                            contribution_meter.step(m.evaluation_steps())?;
                            crate::rows::adoption::charge(contribution_meter, crate::rows::adoption::contribution_bytes(&row, table))?;
                        }
                        for key in index_keys(index, &row, id) {
                            if key.len() > crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES {
                                return Err(crate::rows::index_key_refusal(name, index_name, 1));
                            }
                            if contribution.is_some() { contribution_meter.charge(crate::error::MeterKind::IndexEntries, 1)?; }
                            super::activation_index::insert(&tx, ordered, catalog[&format!("{name}.{index_name}")], &key, id)?;
                        }
                        contribution_meter.release_heap(scratch);
                    }
                    Ok(())
                })?;
                let label = super::adoption_report::table_label(schema, name);
                report.detail(format!("project/index {label} ({} indexes)", plan.indexes.len()), started);
            }
            if ordered {
                let started = std::time::Instant::now();
                tx.conn().execute_batch("INSERT INTO main.i(ti, k, id) SELECT ti, k, id FROM temp._adoption_index ORDER BY ti, k; DROP TABLE temp._adoption_index;")?;
                report.detail("ordered index insertion", started);
            }
        }
        report.phase("project and index", &mut phase);
        // Upgrade old stores and rebuild only when the schema changes. Counts
        // participate in the same activation transaction as rows and indexes.
        if cap_counts && (population_changed || tx.meta("cap_counts_schema")?.as_deref() != Some(&schema_hash)) {
            tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _cap_counts;")?;
            for (name, table) in schema.tables.iter().filter(|(_,t)| t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
                if table.is_expiring() && table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Cap { .. })) {
                    walk(&tx, catalog[name], |_, row| {
                        tx.update_cap_counts(name, table, None, Some(&row)).map(|_| ())
                    })?;
                }
            }
            tx.set_meta("cap_counts_schema", &schema_hash)?;
        }
        report.phase("cap counts", &mut phase);
        build(&tx, changed, &mut report)?;
        if rebuilding || reset || tx.meta(super::time_audience::PUBLICATION_STATE_KEY)?.as_deref() != Some("1") {
            tx.rebuild_time_audiences(schema, now)?;
        }
        report.phase("derived rebuild", &mut phase);
        if rebuilding && programs.is_some() && super::dormant::enabled(&tx)? {
            super::adopt_refusals::candidate(&tx,schema,if population_changed{None}else{previous.as_ref()},true)?;
        }
        if rebuilding {
            for (name, table) in schema.tables.iter().filter(|(_,t)| t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
                let plan = &plans[name];
                // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.6 — physical reuse cannot exempt rebuilt rows from unchanged invariants.
                let rebuilt = schema.maintain_for(name).is_some_and(|m| {
                    previous.as_ref().is_none_or(|old| crate::successor::maintain_rebuild_needed(old, schema, m))
                });
                if plan.project || rebuilt {
                walk(&tx, catalog[name], |_, mut row| {
                    materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                    if programs.is_none() { if let Value::Row(fields) = &mut row { fields.remove("pending"); } }
                    crate::engine::validate::validate_maintained_cache(schema, name, &row)?;
                    for (column, kind) in &table.columns { validate_assets(&tx, kind, row.field(column).ok())?; }
                    Ok(())
                })?;
                }
                for (index_name, index) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(name, i)| {
                    i.unique && (plan.project || rebuilt || plan.indexes.contains(*name))
                }) {
                    validate_groups(&tx, name, index_name, index.components.len(), 1, true)?;
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.2 — a `set null` cap skips its null group; adding or removing that exemption re-validates the cap.
                let exemption_changed = previous.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| old.tables.get(name).is_some_and(|old| old.constraints.iter().any(|c|
                    matches!(c, Constraint::Cap { by, .. } if old.cap_exempts_null(by) != table.cap_exempts_null(by)))));
                for constraint in table.constraints.iter().filter(|_| plan.project || rebuilt || exemption_changed) {
                    if let Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint {
                        let (index_name, _) = table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, i)| i.is_plain() && i.columns().starts_with(by)).ok_or_else(|| {
                                RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{name} cap has no matching index"))
                            })?;
                        validate_groups(&tx, name, index_name, by.len(), u64::from(*max), table.cap_exempts_null(by))?;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        report.phase("validation", &mut phase);
        if rebuilding {
            // A removed asset home is retired in the same transaction as its
            // projection. The ordinary collector removes bytes after commit.
            let homes: Vec<(String, String)> = tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT home_table, home_column FROM _assets WHERE home_table IS NOT NULL AND released_at IS NULL")?.query_map([], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
            for (table, column) in homes {
                if schema.table(&table).and_then(|t| t.columns.get(&column)).is_none_or(|kind| !contains_asset_type(kind)) && !super::dormant::holds_home(&tx,&table,&column)? {
                    tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at = ?3 WHERE home_table = ?1 AND home_column = ?2 AND released_at IS NULL", params![table, column, crate::now_ms()])?;
                }
            }
        }
        let previous = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'generation'", [], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0)).optional()?.and_then(|s| s.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
        let generation = if programs.is_some() && changed { previous.checked_add(1).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))? } else { previous.max(1) };
        tx.set_meta("schema_hash", &schema_hash)?;
        if let Some(hash) = &activation_hash {
            tx.set_meta("index_keys_validated", &format!("{}:{hash}", crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES))?;
        } else {
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'", [])?;
        }
        if let (Some(hash), Some(programs)) = (&activation_hash, programs) {
            tx.set_meta("activation_hash", hash)?;
            tx.set_meta("generation", &generation.to_string())?;
            if changed {
                tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _generations(generation, schema, programs, at) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3, ?4)", params![i64::try_from(generation).map_err(|_| RuntimeError::Generation("generation overflow".into()))?, canonical_json(schema), canonical_json(programs), crate::now_ms()])?;
            }
            // Inspection-only metadata shares the successful activation's
            // transaction, but never its schema bytes, hash or generation.
            if let Some(indexes) = &schema.authored_indexes.0 {
                let record = crate::schema::inspection::Record {
                    generation, schema_hash, indexes: indexes.clone(),
                };
                tx.set_meta(crate::schema::inspection::META_KEY, &canonical_json(&record))?;
            }
        }
        let after = identity(tx.conn())?;
        super::live_activation::finalize(&tx)?;
        let result = finish(&tx)?;
        super::live_activation::cleanup(&tx)?;
        report.phase("metadata", &mut phase);
        tx.commit()?;
        report.phase("commit", &mut phase);
        // Reopening an unchanged backend must not announce a new adoption.
        self.adoption_report = if changed || reset { report } else { Default::default() };
        // Historical ordinals stay in _catalog, but no runtime transaction can address memory tables.
        catalog.retain(|name,_| {
            !schema.tables.iter().any(|(table,t)| {
                t.ephemeral.is_some() && (name == table || name.starts_with(&format!("{table}.")))
            })
        });
        self.catalog = catalog;
        self.identity = after;
        self.accept_open();
        Ok((generation, result))
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.6 — a key-bound proof must be invalidated even by direct SQL edits.
fn index_key_guards(conn: &Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let limit = crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES;
    let insert = format!("_index_keys_insert_{limit}");
    let update = format!("_index_keys_update_{limit}");
    let installed: i64 = conn.query_row(
        "SELECT count(*) FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type = 'trigger' AND name IN (?1, ?2)",
        params![insert, update], |r| r.get(0),
    )?;
    if installed != 2 {
        // A predecessor without both guards could have retained a proof across
        // physical edits. Install and revalidate in this activation transaction.
        conn.execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'", [])?;
        conn.execute_batch(&format!(
            "CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS {insert} AFTER INSERT ON i WHEN length(NEW.k) > {limit}
             BEGIN DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'; END;
             CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS {update} AFTER UPDATE OF k ON i WHEN length(NEW.k) > {limit}
             BEGIN DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key = 'index_keys_validated'; END;"
        ))?;
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Equal prefixes are adjacent in an index. Validate uniqueness and caps
/// in one bounded pass per invariant, retaining one key rather than one
/// allocation for every group/row in a potentially large activation.
fn validate_groups(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: &str, index: &str, prefix_len: usize, max: u64, nulls_distinct: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::word_heap as heap};
    // Only encoded group keys matter. Fetching source images here would
    // recreate an unmetered 500-row payload buffer after the metered rebuild.
    let mut statement = tx.conn().prepare_cached("SELECT k FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 ORDER BY k")?;
    let mut rows = statement.query([tx.num(&format!("{table}.{index}"))?])?;
    let mut previous: Option<Vec<Value>> = None;
    let mut count = 0;
    let mut visited = 0;
    let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
    while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
        if visited % 256 == 0 {
            meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
            meter.at(&format!("adopt {table}.{index} groups"));
            meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        }
        visited += 1;
        let scratch = meter.mark();
        meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
        let key = row.get_ref(0)?.as_blob().map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
        meter.heap(heap::key_decode(key.len(), key.len()))?;
        if let Some(values) = &previous {
            meter.heap(heap::slots::<Value>(values.len()))?;
            crate::engine::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut meter, |meter, scope| {
                for value in values { value.retained_internal(scope).charge(meter, scope)?; }
                Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(())
            })?;
        }
        let mut group = crate::key::decode(key).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("invalid rebuilt index key".into()))?;
        group.truncate(prefix_len);
        if !(nulls_distinct && group.contains(&Value::Null)) {
            if previous.as_ref() == Some(&group) { count += 1; } else { previous = Some(group); count = 1; }
            if count > max { return Err(RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("cannot activate {table}.{index}: existing group exceeds {}", if nulls_distinct { "a unique key".to_owned() } else { format!("cap {max}") }))); }
        }
        meter.release_heap(scratch);
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn contains_asset_type(kind: &crate::schema::ColumnType) -> bool {
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match kind { Optional(inner) | List { of: inner, .. } | ListRange { of: inner, .. } => {
            contains_asset_type(inner)
        }
        Image { .. } | Video { .. } | File { .. } => true, _ => false }
}
pub(super) fn validate_assets(tx: &Tx<'_>, kind: &crate::schema::ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    use crate::assets::AssetKind;
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match (kind, value) {
        (Optional(inner), Some(value)) => validate_assets(tx, inner, Some(value)),
        (List { of, .. } | ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => { for value in values { validate_assets(tx, of, Some(value))?; } Ok(()) }
        (Image { .. } | Video { .. } | File { .. }, Some(value)) => {
            let (wanted, max, types) = kind.asset_leaf().expect("an asset leaf");
            if let Some(id) = crate::assets::asset_id(Some(value)) {
                if let Some(asset) = tx.asset(id)? {
                    if asset.kind != wanted { return Err(RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TYPE", message: format!("cannot activate: {id} is {}, not {}", asset.kind.word(), wanted.word()) }); }
                    if wanted == AssetKind::File && !crate::assets::file_accepts(types, &asset.content_type) { return Err(RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TYPE", message: format!("cannot activate: {id} is {}, which the column no longer accepts", asset.content_type) }); }
                    if asset.bytes > u64::from(max) { return Err(RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TOO_LARGE", message: format!("cannot activate: {id} has {} bytes; the column takes at most {max}", asset.bytes) }); }
                }
            }
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(())
    }
}
