//! Database-authoritative release history and explicit dormant pruning.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#4-explicit-pruning-and-audit — no implicit collector or unaudited data loss.
use super::*;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct DormantItem {
    /// Stable cursor; column/index names may coincide.
    pub key: String,
    pub path: String,
    pub kind: String,
    pub action: String,
    pub rows: u64,
    pub bytes: u64,
    pub asset_bytes: u64,
    /// Internal audit distinction: zero standalone cells may still be covered by a dormant row.
    #[serde(skip)]
    pub(crate) definition_only: bool,
    pub sample_ids: Vec<String>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub before_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub after_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub restored_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub before_sample_ids:Option<Vec<String>>,
}

impl DormantItem {
    pub fn unit(&self) -> &'static str {
        match (self.kind.as_str(), self.rows) { ("backfill", 1) => "preimage", ("backfill", _) => "preimages", ("column", 1) => "cell", ("column", _) => "cells", ("index", 1) => "entry", ("index", _) => "entries", (_, 1) => "row", _ => "rows" }
    }
}

impl Store {
    /// Values presently in active rows, independently of table population or other columns.
    pub fn rollback_incompatible_values(&self, current: &Schema, target: &Schema, name: &str, column: &str) -> Result<(u64, Vec<String>), RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.authority_transaction()?;
        let table = &target.tables[name];
        let kind = &table.columns[column];
        if table.engine_field(column) || table.maintained.contains_key(column) { return Ok((0, Vec::new())); }
        let mut count = 0;
        let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        let mut statement = tx.conn().prepare("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=(SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1) ORDER BY id")?;
        for data in statement.query_map([name], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0))? {
            let mut row: Value = serde_json::from_str(&data?).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, &current.tables[name]);
            if let Ok(value) = row.field(column)
                && super::narrowed::incompatible_column(&tx, table, name, column, kind, value)? {
                count += 1;
                if samples.len() < 5 {
                    let text = match value { Value::String(s) => format!("'{}'", s.replace('\\', "\\\\").replace('\'', "\\'").replace('\n', "\\n").replace('\r', "\\r")), _ => canonical_json(value) };
                    samples.insert(super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&text));
                }
            }
        }
        Ok((count, samples.into_iter().collect()))
    }
}

fn sql_number(n: u64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    i64::try_from(n).map_err(|_| {
        RuntimeError::Generation("release counter exceeds SQLite integer range".into())
    })
}

pub(super) fn present(conn: &Connection) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(conn.query_row(
        "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type='table' AND name='_dormant_tables')",
        [],
        |r| r.get(0),
    )?)
}
pub(crate) fn inventory(
    conn: &Connection,
    after: Option<&str>,
    limit: usize,
) -> Result<Vec<DormantItem>, RuntimeError> {
    if !present(conn)? {
        return Ok(Vec::new());
    }
    let narrowed = if super::narrowed::present(conn)? {
        " UNION ALL SELECT c.name,'rows',c.name,NULL FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t GROUP BY n.t "
    } else {""};
    let backfills=super::backfills::inventory_sql(conn)?;
    let query=format!("SELECT path,kind,table_name,column_name FROM (
        SELECT table_name AS path,'table' AS kind,table_name,NULL AS column_name FROM _dormant_tables
        UNION ALL SELECT table_name||'.'||column_name,'column',table_name,column_name FROM _dormant_columns c WHERE table_name NOT IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog t ON t.num=v.t WHERE t.name=c.table_name AND v.column_name=c.column_name)
        UNION ALL SELECT table_name||'.'||index_name,'index',table_name,index_name FROM _dormant_indexes {narrowed} {backfills})
        WHERE (?1 IS NULL OR kind||':'||path>?1) ORDER BY kind||':'||path LIMIT ?2");
    let mut statement=conn.prepare(&query)?;
    let items: Vec<(String, String, String, Option<String>)> = statement
        .query_map(
            params![after, i64::try_from(limit).unwrap_or(i64::MAX)],
            |r| Ok((r.get(0)?, r.get(1)?, r.get(2)?, r.get(3)?)),
        )?
        .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for (path, kind, name, column) in items {
        let t: Option<i64> = conn
            .query_row("SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1", [&name], |r| {
                r.get(0)
            })
            .optional()?;
        let Some(t) = t else { continue };
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — dormant row images cover their retained cells; standalone cells have active owners.
        let (mut count,mut bytes):(u64,u64)=match kind.as_str(){
            "backfill"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(inputs)+octet_length(before_image)+octet_length(after_image)),0) FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND definition=?2",params![t,column],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            "table"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            "rows"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            "column"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_values v WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id)",params![t,column],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            _=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),0 FROM (SELECT id FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1)",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
        };
        if matches!(kind.as_str(), "table" | "rows") {
            let images = if kind == "table" { "_dormant_rows" } else { "_dormant_narrow_rows" };
            bytes += conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(v.id)+octet_length(v.data)),0) FROM _dormant_values v WHERE v.t=?1 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM {images} d WHERE d.t=v.t AND d.id=v.id)"), [t], |r| r.get::<_, i64>(0))? as u64;
        }
        let query = match kind.as_str() {
            "backfill" => "SELECT id FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND definition=?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT 5",
            "rows" => "SELECT id FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5",
            "table" => "SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5",
            "column" => {
                "SELECT id FROM _dormant_values v WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id) ORDER BY id LIMIT 5"
            }
            _ => {
                "SELECT id FROM (SELECT id FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1) WHERE ?2 IS NOT NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5"
            }
        };
        let mut ids = conn
            .prepare(query)?
            .query_map(params![t, column], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0))?
            .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?
            .into_iter()
            .map(|id| super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&id))
            .collect();
        if kind == "index" {
            (count, ids) = index_entries(conn, t, &name, column.as_deref().unwrap())?;
        }
        let asset_bytes = if kind == "index" || kind == "backfill" {
            0
        } else {
            let selector=match kind.as_str(){
                "rows"=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=?3 AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",
                "column"=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN r ON r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id WHERE v.t=?3 AND v.id=_assets.home_id AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column)",
                _=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_rows d WHERE d.t=?3 AND d.id=_assets.home_id)",
            };
            conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(bytes),0) FROM _assets WHERE home_table=?1 AND (?2 IS NULL OR home_column=?2) AND released_at IS NULL AND {selector}"),params![name,if kind=="table" || kind=="rows" {None}else{column.as_deref()},t],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?
        };
        let definition_only = if kind == "column" {
            !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2)", params![t, column], |r| r.get::<_, bool>(0))?
        } else { count == 0 };
        out.push(DormantItem {
            key: format!("{kind}:{path}"),
            path,
            kind,
            action: "dormant".into(),
            rows: count,
            bytes,
            asset_bytes,
            definition_only,
            sample_ids: ids,
            before_rows:None,after_rows:None,restored_rows:None,before_sample_ids:None,
        });
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// Retained definitions represent logical entries, rebuilt on restoration.
/// A words index has zero or many entries per image; a row count is not its size.
fn index_entries(conn: &Connection, t: i64, table: &str, index: &str) -> Result<(u64, Vec<String>), RuntimeError> {
    let definition: String = conn.query_row("SELECT definition FROM _dormant_indexes WHERE table_name=?1 AND index_name=?2", params![table, index], |r| r.get(0))?;
    let index: crate::schema::Index = serde_json::from_str(&definition).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
    let schema: Option<String> = conn.query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')", [], |r| r.get(0)).optional()?;
    let schema: Option<Schema> = schema.map(|s| serde_json::from_str(&s).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))).transpose()?;
    let query = if super::narrowed::present(conn)? {
        "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY id"
    } else { "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY id" };
    let mut count = 0;
    let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let mut statement = conn.prepare(query)?;
    for image in statement.query_map([t], |r| Ok((r.get::<_, String>(0)?, r.get::<_, String>(1)?)))? {
        let (id, data) = image?;
        let mut row: Value = serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
        if let Some(table) = schema.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.table(table)) { crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table); }
        for cell in conn.prepare("SELECT column_name,data FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2")?.query_map(params![t, id], |r| Ok((r.get::<_, String>(0)?, r.get::<_, String>(1)?)))? {
            let (column, value) = cell?;
            if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
                fields.entry(column).or_insert(serde_json::from_str(&value).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?);
            }
        }
        let entries = crate::rows::index_keys(&index, &row, &id).len() as u64;
        count += entries;
        if entries > 0 && samples.len() < 5 { samples.insert(super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&id)); }
    }
    Ok((count, samples.into_iter().collect()))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#91-per-row-narrowed-value-dormancy — only rows surviving every activation phase count as restored.
pub(crate) fn finalize_restorations(tx:&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    if super::live_activation::active(tx)? { return super::live_activation::finalize(tx); }
    let entries=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT key,value FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored_ids:%'")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,String>(0)?,r.get::<_,String>(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>()?;
    let mut narrowed=0u64;
    for (key,ids) in entries {
        let (prefix,path)=key.split_once(':').expect("activation restoration key");
        let table=if prefix=="activation_column_restored_ids" {path.split_once('.').expect("column path").0} else {path};
        let count:u64=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?2))",params![tx.num(table)?,ids],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?;
        tx.set_meta(&format!("{}:{path}",prefix.trim_end_matches("_ids")),&count.to_string())?;
        if prefix=="activation_narrowed_restored_ids" {narrowed+=count;}
    }
    tx.set_meta("activation_narrowed_restored",&narrowed.to_string())?;
    Ok(())
}

pub(crate) fn event(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    operation: &str,
    release: Option<u64>,
    previous: Option<u64>,
    summary: serde_json::Value,
    items: &[DormantItem],
) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
    let revision = tx
        .meta("release_revision")?
        .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
        .unwrap_or(0)
        .checked_add(1)
        .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("release revision exhausted".into()))?;
    tx.set_meta("release_revision", &revision.to_string())?;
    let generation = tx
        .meta("generation")?
        .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
        .unwrap_or(1);
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _release_events(at,operation,release,previous,generation,revision,summary) VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7)",params![crate::now_ms(),operation,release.map(sql_number).transpose()?,previous.map(sql_number).transpose()?,sql_number(generation)?,sql_number(revision)?,summary.to_string()])?;
    let id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
    for item in items {
        tx.conn().execute(
            "INSERT INTO _release_event_items VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",
            params![id, item.key, canonical_json(item)],
        )?;
    }
    Ok(revision)
}

impl Store {
    pub fn dormant_inventory(
        &mut self,
        after: Option<&str>,
        limit: usize,
    ) -> Result<Vec<DormantItem>, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let items = inventory(tx.conn(), after, limit)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(items)
    }
    /// One snapshot: current identity, bounded history and storage inventories.
    pub fn release_status(
        &mut self,
        after: u64,
        items_after: Option<&str>,
        event_id: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let mut events = Vec::new();
        let mut inventory_page = Vec::<serde_json::Value>::new();
        if present(tx.conn())? {
            let mut s=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT id,at,operation,release,previous,generation,revision,summary FROM _release_events WHERE id>?1 ORDER BY id LIMIT 16")?;
            let mut rows = s.query([sql_number(after)?])?;
            while let Some(r) = rows.next()? {
                events.push(serde_json::json!({"id":r.get::<_,i64>(0)?,"at":r.get::<_,i64>(1)?,"operation":r.get::<_,String>(2)?,"release":r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(3)?,"previous":r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(4)?,"generation":r.get::<_,i64>(5)?,"revision":r.get::<_,i64>(6)?,"summary":serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&r.get::<_,String>(7)?).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?}));
            }
            if let Some(event) = event_id {
                let mut s=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT detail FROM _release_event_items WHERE event=?1 AND (?2 IS NULL OR path>?2) ORDER BY path LIMIT 16")?;
                for item in s.query_map(params![sql_number(event)?, items_after], |r| {
                    r.get::<_, String>(0)
                })? {
                    inventory_page.push(
                        serde_json::from_str(&item?)
                            .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?,
                    );
                }
            } else {
                inventory_page = inventory(tx.conn(), items_after, 16)?
                    .into_iter()
                    .map(|i| serde_json::to_value(i).unwrap())
                    .collect();
            }
        }
        let workers:Vec<(String,String)>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT key,value FROM _meta WHERE key='worker:status' OR key LIKE 'worker:status:%' ORDER BY key LIMIT 16")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        let previous_active: Option<u64> = if present(tx.conn())? {tx.conn().query_row("SELECT previous FROM _release_events WHERE release IS NOT NULL AND previous != release AND operation != 'prune' ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1",[],|r|r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(0)).optional()?.flatten().map(|n|n as u64)} else {None};
        let result = serde_json::json!({"previous_active":previous_active,"release":tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()),"revision":tx.meta("release_revision")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),"generation":tx.meta("generation")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(1),"native_digest":tx.meta("release_native_digest")?,"events_after":events.last().map(|e|e["id"].clone()),"events":events,"items_after":inventory_page.last().map(|i|i["key"].clone()),"items":inventory_page,"event":event_id,"workers":workers});
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(result)
    }
    pub fn prune_dormant(
        &mut self,
        confirm: bool,
        expected_revision: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.transaction()?;
        let held = tx
            .meta("release_revision")?
            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
            .unwrap_or(0);
        if expected_revision.is_some_and(|n| n != held) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation(
                "release revision changed; run releases and retry prune".into(),
            ));
        }
        let items = inventory(tx.conn(), None, usize::MAX)?;
        if !confirm {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(
                serde_json::json!({"pruned":false,"items":items,"rewrite":"snapback4 prune <dir> --allow-destructive"}),
            );
        }
        if !present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":[],"revision":held}));
        }
        // Release only dormant homes; active application data is untouched.
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE home_table IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=v.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column AND v.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
        if super::narrowed::present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=_dormant_values.t AND n.id=_dormant_values.id)",[])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_narrow_rows",[])?;
        }
        let revision = event(
            &tx,
            "prune",
            tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v| v.parse().ok()),
            None,
            serde_json::json!({"dropped_items":items.len(),"bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.bytes).sum::<u64>(),"asset_bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.asset_bytes).sum::<u64>()}),
            &items,
        )?;
        let event_id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
        super::backfills::prune(&tx)?;
        tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _dormant_values;DELETE FROM _dormant_rows;DELETE FROM _dormant_refs;DELETE FROM _dormant_columns;DELETE FROM _dormant_indexes;DELETE FROM _dormant_tables;")?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":items,"revision":revision,"event":event_id}))
    }
}

/// The retirement phase shared by activation and its read-only occupancy
/// preview. Selection and the predecessor deadline predicate live here once;
/// checking a candidate must not count a row this phase would remove.
pub(crate) struct Retirement<'a> {
    affected: Vec<(String, &'a Table)>,
    now: i64,
}

impl<'a> Retirement<'a> {
    pub(crate) fn new(next: &Schema, previous: &'a Schema, now: i64) -> Self {
        let affected = next
            .through_order()
            .into_iter()
            .filter_map(|name| {
                let next = &next.tables[&name];
                let old = previous.table(&name)?;
                Self::changes_lifetime(next,old).then_some((name, old))
            })
            .collect();
        Self { affected, now }
    }

    fn changes_lifetime(next:&Table,old:&Table) -> bool {
        next.through != old.through || next.through.is_some() && (next.expire != old.expire || next.expire.as_ref().is_some_and(|field|old.defaults.get(field)!=next.defaults.get(field)))
    }

    pub(crate) fn retires_image(next:&Table,old:&Table,row:&Value,now:i64) -> bool {
        (next.through!=old.through || next.expire!=old.expire || old.expire.as_ref().is_some_and(|field|old.defaults.get(field)!=next.defaults.get(field))) && old.expired(row,now)
    }

    pub(crate) fn affects(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        self.affected.iter().any(|(table, _)| table == name)
    }

    pub(crate) fn retires(&self, name: &str, row: &Value) -> bool {
        self.affected
            .iter()
            .find(|(table, _)| table == name)
            .is_some_and(|(_, old)| old.expired(row, self.now))
    }
}

/// Capture barrier for an authoritative adapter's uncommitted generation.
pub fn activate(
    tx: &dyn Rows,
    next: &Schema,

    now: i64,
) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, u64>, RuntimeError> {
    activate_retained(tx, next, previous, now, &Default::default())
}

//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
use super::*;
use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};

pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
}

/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    loop {
        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        let page_mark = meter.mark();
        let mut limit = PAGE;
        let mut page = loop {
            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
                    bytes = next;
                    count += 1;
                }
            }
            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
            let query = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
            }
            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
            drop(page);
            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
        };
        for (id, data) in &page {
            let row_mark = meter.mark();
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
        }
        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
        drop(page);
        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    }
}

# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```
For a complete real-user staging script and first-admin setup, run `guide staging`. Bulk account setup is restricted to fresh `deploy --staging` stores. Generations climb on every actual switch, including rollback and forward; selecting identical execution content is a no-op. `token --service ops --release ./deployment` works while serve runs. Signups are rate-limited: create proof accounts sequentially and respect a returned retry delay. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development and classified staging proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`); production always uses wall time.

## Upgrade
`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.

## Deploy and password identity
Add `use identity password` (included below). Deploy from the app directory onto durable storage:
Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.
```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```
Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness.

Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.

## First admin: a service seat
Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  sync
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```
```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```
This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads.

A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or a declarative `backfill table name(fields: types): {column: expression}`; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
```
`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused candidate is never selectable. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Identical source is a no-op. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. A dev/older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying; failed adoption keeps current.

Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; rollback selects restoration explicitly. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## Bridge a safety gate before rollback
Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.

1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.

## What the old release sees
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
```js
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1'); const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
assert.equal(absent.status, 404); assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest'); assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');

    /// (LLP 3000.039 §2.2), so it proves no population bound.
    pub fn cap_exempts_null(&self, by: &[String]) -> bool {
        by.iter().any(|column| self.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&references::OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

    pub fn immutable_fields(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
        self.constraints.iter().flat_map(|constraint| match constraint {
            Constraint::Immutable { fields } => fields.iter().map(String::as_str).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            _ => Vec::new(),
        })
    }

    /// Whether `row` is past its `expire` column at `now`: absent to every
    /// reader, pending the sweep. A null time never expires.
    pub fn expired(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.deadline(row).is_some_and(|at| at <= now)
    }

    /// The index the expiry sweep walks: the first leading with the
    /// `expire` column, as the declaration requires.
    pub fn expiry_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.expiry_column()?;
        self.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            }).map(|(name, index)| (name.as_str(), index))
    }

    /// The column carrying the principal a `sync to T[...]` audience reads.
    pub fn principal_columns(&self) -> Vec<&str> {
        Value::Array(items) => items.iter().any(contains_system_principal),
        Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) => fields.values().any(contains_system_principal),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Missing optional columns read as null; missing required columns with a
/// literal default read that value. Contextual defaults belong to inserts.
pub fn materialize_optional_fields(row: &mut Value, table: &Table) {
    let fields = match row {
        Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) => fields,
        _ => return,
    };
    for (name, kind) in &table.columns {
        if !fields.contains_key(name) {
            if table.maintained.contains_key(name) {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), Value::Int(0));
            } else if kind.is_optional() {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
            } else if let Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(value)) = table.defaults.get(name) {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), value.clone());
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Borrow complete images; materialize old images only when needed.
pub fn row_image<'a>(row: &'a Value, table: &Table) -> std::borrow::Cow<'a, Value> {
    if table.columns.keys().all(|column| row.field(column).is_ok()) { return std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(row); }
    let mut image = row.clone();
    materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, table);
    std::borrow::Cow::Owned(image)
}

/// The type an argument declares as a column type, for value validation.
pub fn arg_as_column(kind: &ArgType) -> Option<ColumnType> {
    Some(match kind {
        ArgType::Cursor | ArgType::Scalar => return None,
        ArgType::Id => ColumnType::Id,
        ArgType::Principal => ColumnType::Principal,
        ArgType::Ref { table } => ColumnType::Ref { table: table.clone() },
        ArgType::Text { max, .. } => ColumnType::Text { max: *max },
        ArgType::Bytes { max } => ColumnType::Bytes { max: *max },
        ArgType::Image { max } => ColumnType::Image { max: *max, once: false },
//! Server-authoritative lower row lifetime, independent of physical expiry.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.003#2-one-lifetime-predicate — every reader shares this half-open lifetime.
use super::*;

/// One opening is one system commit. Structural admission keeps that commit
/// inside the ordinary engine heap and event-count envelopes before any row
/// can enter the pending ledger.
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_EVENTS: u64 = 1_024;
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_BYTES: u64 = 16 << 20;

impl Table {
    /// Reads must classify/filter stored images before public actual-size
    /// accounting whenever either lifetime edge can hide one.
    pub fn read_lifetime_sensitive(&self) -> bool {
        self.not_before.is_some() || self.is_expiring()
    }

    pub fn opens_at(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        self.not_before
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|field| match row.field(field) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                Ok(_) => None,
                Err(_) => match self.defaults.get(field) {
                    Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(Value::Int(at))) => Some(*at),
                    _ => None,
                },
            })
    }
    pub fn opened(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opens_at(row).is_none_or(|at| at <= now)
    }
    pub fn live(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opened(row, now) && !self.expired(row, now)
    }
    pub fn opening_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.not_before.as_deref()?;
        self.indexes
            .iter()
            .find(|(_, index)| {
                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            })
            .map(|(name, index)| (name.as_str(), index))
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let timed = |name: &str| {
        schema
            .table(name)
            .is_some_and(|table| table.not_before.is_some())
    };
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        let path = format!("tables.{name}");
        if table.not_before.is_some() && table.ephemeral.is_some() {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.ephemeral"), message:
                "ephemeral: not before cannot appear on an ephemeral table; keep the scheduled payload in a durable table".into() });
        }
        if let Some(through) = &table.through
            && (table.not_before.is_some() || timed(&through.parent))
        {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message:
                format!("not before cannot compose with read through: {name} and {} need one captured opening boundary; use an explicit durable table", through.parent) });
        }
        if table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(Sync::is_union) {
            let mut scopes = vec![name.as_str()];
            scopes.extend(
                table
                    .sync
                    .as_ref()
                    .into_iter()
                    .flat_map(Sync::arms)
                    .filter_map(|(audience, _)| match audience {
                        Audience::Target { table, .. } => Some(table.as_str()),
                        _ => None,
                    }),
            );
            if let Some(scope) = scopes.into_iter().find(|scope| timed(scope)) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.sync"), message:
                    format!("union audiences cannot use a time audience: {scope} has `not before`; use `online only`") });
            }
        }
        if table.not_before.is_some() {
            let own = table
                .sync
                .as_ref()
                .map_or(0, |sync| sync.arms().count() as u64)
                + table
                    .written_by
                    .values()
                    .filter(|authority| !authority.audience)
                    .count() as u64;
            let memberships = schema.tables.values().flat_map(|content| content.sync.iter().flat_map(Sync::arms))
                .filter(|(audience, _)| matches!(audience, Audience::Target { table: target, .. } if target == name))
                .count() as u64;
            let events = own.saturating_add(memberships);
            let owner_slots = table
                .written_by
                .values()
                .filter(|authority| !authority.audience)
                .count() as u64;
            let mut read_probes = Vec::new();
            crate::engine::rules::probe_sites(&table.rules.read, &mut read_probes);
            // The opening row is decoded before publication, including for an
            // online-only table which emits no event. A join carries a subset
            // of that row's bounded fields plus less framing than this same
            // envelope. Reserve at least the base decode and one envelope for
            // every possible event.
            let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table)
                .saturating_mul(events.max(1));
            if events > MAX_OPENING_EVENTS || bytes > MAX_OPENING_BYTES {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message: format!(
                    "not before opening fanout is too large for one bounded publication commit ({events} events, {bytes} reserved bytes); split this audience into smaller tables"
                ) });
            }
            // Opening runs as the system principal, so its recipient-specific
            // private-field projection cannot safely borrow the caller's
            // bounded authorization walk or scratch. Keep that projection
            // local to the opening row; ordinary rows may still use witnesses.
            if owner_slots > 0 && !read_probes.is_empty() {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message: format!(
                    "not before with private `written by` fields needs a probe-free read rule so opening publication has no unbounded witness walk; use local field comparisons, declare the fields readable by audience, or remove `not before`"
                ) });
            }
        }
            .filter(|c| !table.columns.contains_key(*c))
        {
            definition(tx, name, old, column)?;
        }
        super::activation::walk(tx, t, |id, row| {
            let removed: Vec<_> = row
                .as_object()?
                .iter()
                .filter(|(c, _)| !table.columns.contains_key(*c))
                .map(|(c, v)| (c.clone(), v.clone()))
                .collect();
            for (column, value) in removed {
                if old.columns.contains_key(&column) {
                    save_cell(tx, name, old, id, &column, &value, false)?;
                }
            }

            // Full source image/deadlines stay intact until lifetime retirement and narrowed classification.
            Ok(())
        })?;
        if super::live_activation::active(tx)? {
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored SELECT 'table',t,'',id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1",[t])?;

On candidate restoration, evidence invalidated by an actual deletion keeps a row dormant only if its reference/capture column is candidate-visible. Deletion invalidates only bound (invalid=0) edges. Omitted reference cells and evidence stay retained for future restoration. A narrowed row never rebinds a deleted visible parent. Whole-table restoration keeps the full source image through lifetime retirement and narrowed classification; ordinary candidate projection follows that boundary. Restoration identity sets are held transactionally and counted against final surviving r only after all read-through/cache activation phases. There is no automatic collection.

//! Private filesystem-owner control transport; never an HTTP application door.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — the host remains the only database writer.
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::unix::{
    ffi::OsStrExt,
    fs::{DirBuilderExt, FileTypeExt, MetadataExt, OpenOptionsExt, PermissionsExt},
    net::{UnixListener, UnixStream},
};
use std::{
    fs,
    io::{self, Read, Write},
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
    time::{Duration, Instant},
};

const INPUT: usize = 4096;
const OUTPUT: usize = 64 * 1024;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(tag = "command", rename_all = "kebab-case", deny_unknown_fields)]
pub(crate) enum Command {
    Status {
        after: u64,
        items_after: Option<String>,
        event: Option<u64>,
    },
    Activate {
        release: u64,
        expected_release: Option<u64>,
        expected_revision: u64,
        digest: String,
        rollback: bool,
        restore_dormant: bool,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    },
    Prune {
        confirm: bool,
        expected_revision: u64,
    },
}
#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Envelope {
    identity: String,
    command: Command,
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn identity(store: &Path) -> Result<String, String> {
    let path = store.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let m = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&path)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
        .ok_or("store disappeared")?;
    Ok(crate::native::digest(
        &serde_json::to_vec(&(path.as_os_str().as_bytes(), m.dev(), m.ino())).unwrap(),
    ))
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn peer(stream: &UnixStream) -> io::Result<()> {
    use std::os::fd::AsRawFd;
    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    let uid = {
        let mut credentials = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<libc::ucred>::uninit();
        let mut size = std::mem::size_of::<libc::ucred>() as libc::socklen_t;
        if unsafe {
            libc::getsockopt(
                stream.as_raw_fd(),
                libc::SOL_SOCKET,
                libc::SO_PEERCRED,
                credentials.as_mut_ptr().cast(),
                &mut size,
            )
        } != 0
        {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        if size as usize != std::mem::size_of::<libc::ucred>() {
            return Err(io::Error::other("invalid peer credentials"));
        }
        unsafe { credentials.assume_init() }.uid
    };
    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
    let uid = {
        let (mut uid, mut gid) = (0, 0);
        if unsafe { libc::getpeereid(stream.as_raw_fd(), &mut uid, &mut gid) } != 0 {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        uid
    };
    #[cfg(not(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")))]
    return Err(io::Error::other(
        "release control peer credentials unsupported on this Unix host",
    ));
    #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
    if uid != unsafe { libc::geteuid() } {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control requires the same filesystem owner",
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn socket_entry(path: &Path) -> io::Result<fs::Metadata> {
    let m = fs::symlink_metadata(path)?;
    if !m.file_type().is_socket()
        || m.uid() != unsafe { libc::geteuid() }
        || m.permissions().mode() & 0o7777 != 0o600
    {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control must be a same-owner 0600 socket, never a symlink or regular file",
        ));
    }
    Ok(m)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#51-path-independent-control-address — fixed short runtime root, independent of shell environment.
#[cfg(unix)]
fn private_directory(path: &Path, create: bool) -> io::Result<()> {
    if create {
        match fs::DirBuilder::new().mode(0o700).create(path) {
            Ok(()) => {}
            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists => {}
            Err(e) => return Err(e),
        }
    }
    // Do not chmod or follow an existing entry. A stranger can pre-create a
    // name in /tmp, but cannot turn that into a capability or chmod target.
    let directory = fs::OpenOptions::new()
        .read(true)
        .custom_flags(libc::O_DIRECTORY | libc::O_NOFOLLOW)
        .open(path)?;
    directory_metadata(&directory.metadata()?, unsafe { libc::geteuid() })
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn directory_metadata(m: &fs::Metadata, uid: u32) -> io::Result<()> {
    if !m.is_dir() || m.uid() != uid || m.permissions().mode() & 0o7777 != 0o700 {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control runtime directory must be a same-owner 0700 directory",
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn address(dir: &Path, create: bool) -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
    let directory = PathBuf::from(format!("/tmp/snapback4-{}", unsafe { libc::geteuid() }));
    private_directory(&directory, create)?;
    let canonical = dir.canonicalize()?;
    // Bind the address to immutable deployment source, even when two
    // deployments symlink their database to the same store. The actual
    // database path/inode is separately checked in every envelope.
    Ok(directory.join(format!(
        "{}.sock",
        crate::native::digest(canonical.as_os_str().as_bytes())
    )))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#51-path-independent-control-address — a total input deadline bounds shutdown even when a peer keeps sending bytes.
#[cfg(unix)]
fn input(stream: &mut UnixStream, timeout: Duration) -> io::Result<Vec<u8>> {
    let deadline = Instant::now() + timeout;
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    let mut buffer = [0; INPUT + 1];
    while bytes.len() <= INPUT {
        let remaining = deadline.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now());
        if remaining.is_zero() {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::TimedOut,
                "release control input deadline exceeded",
            ));
        }
        stream.set_read_timeout(Some(remaining))?;
        match stream.read(&mut buffer[..INPUT + 1 - bytes.len()]) {
            Ok(0) => break,
            Ok(n) => bytes.extend_from_slice(&buffer[..n]),
            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::Interrupted => continue,
            Err(e) => return Err(e),
        }
    }
    Ok(bytes)

//! Lifetime-bound rule inputs, distinct from historical recorded attribution.
// @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — captures have their parent's type and activation lifecycle.
use super::*;

pub const PARENT_DEADLINE: &str = "$parentDeadline";
pub const DEADLINE: &str = "$deadline";
pub const CHILDREN: &str = "$children";
pub const LIVE_CHILDREN: &str = "$liveChildren";

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Through {
    /// Bounded live maintenance; absence preserves captured wire bytes.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub live: Option<u32>,
    pub reference: String,
    pub parent: String,
    /// Child engine field -> immediate parent field (including its captures).
    pub fields: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub identity: String,
    pub expiring: bool,
}

impl Table {
    pub fn engine_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        (self.ephemeral.is_some() && field == DEADLINE) || self.recorded.contains_key(field)
            || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| {
                t.fields.contains_key(field) || matches!(field, DEADLINE | PARENT_DEADLINE)
            })
    }
    /// One classification for every expiry gate, including inherited deadlines.
    pub fn is_expiring(&self) -> bool {
        self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.expire.is_some() || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring)
    }
    pub fn expiry_column(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        if self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.through.is_some() && self.is_expiring() {
            Some(DEADLINE)
        } else {
            self.expire.as_deref()
        }
    }
    pub fn deadline(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        match self
            .expiry_column()
            .and_then(|field| row_image(row, self).field(field).ok().cloned())
        {
            Some(Value::Int(at)) => Some(at),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    /// Never trust a restored effective instant; recompute from immutable capture.
    pub fn recompute_deadline(&self, row: &mut Value) -> Result<(), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
        if self.through.is_none() {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let authored = self
            .expire
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|c| match row_image(row, self).field(c) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                _ => None,
            });
        let parent = match row.field(PARENT_DEADLINE)? {
            Value::Int(at) => Some(*at),
            Value::Null => None,
            _ => {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Input(
                    "captured parent deadline must be a time or null".into(),
                ));
            }
        };
        let deadline = authored
            .into_iter()
            .chain(parent)
            .min()
            .map(Value::Int)
            .unwrap_or(Value::Null);
        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = row {
            fields.insert(DEADLINE.into(), deadline);
        }
        Ok(())

//! Private filesystem-owner control transport; never an HTTP application door.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — the host remains the only database writer.
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::unix::{
    ffi::OsStrExt,
    fs::{DirBuilderExt, FileTypeExt, MetadataExt, OpenOptionsExt, PermissionsExt},
    net::{UnixListener, UnixStream},
};
use std::{
    fs,
    io::{self, Read, Write},
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
    time::{Duration, Instant},
};

const INPUT: usize = 4096;
const OUTPUT: usize = 64 * 1024;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(tag = "command", rename_all = "kebab-case", deny_unknown_fields)]
pub(crate) enum Command {
    Status {
        after: u64,
        items_after: Option<String>,
        event: Option<u64>,
    },
    Activate {
        release: u64,
        expected_release: Option<u64>,
        expected_revision: u64,
        digest: String,
        rollback: bool,
        restore_dormant: bool,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    },
    Prune {
        confirm: bool,
        expected_revision: u64,
    },
}
#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Envelope {
    identity: String,
    command: Command,
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn identity(store: &Path) -> Result<String, String> {
    let path = store.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let m = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&path)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
        .ok_or("store disappeared")?;
    Ok(crate::native::digest(
        &serde_json::to_vec(&(path.as_os_str().as_bytes(), m.dev(), m.ino())).unwrap(),
    ))
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn peer(stream: &UnixStream) -> io::Result<()> {
    use std::os::fd::AsRawFd;
    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    let uid = {
        let mut credentials = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<libc::ucred>::uninit();
        let mut size = std::mem::size_of::<libc::ucred>() as libc::socklen_t;
        if unsafe {
            libc::getsockopt(
                stream.as_raw_fd(),
                libc::SOL_SOCKET,
                libc::SO_PEERCRED,
                credentials.as_mut_ptr().cast(),
                &mut size,
            )
        } != 0
        {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        if size as usize != std::mem::size_of::<libc::ucred>() {
            return Err(io::Error::other("invalid peer credentials"));
        }
        unsafe { credentials.assume_init() }.uid
    };
    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
    let uid = {
        let (mut uid, mut gid) = (0, 0);
        if unsafe { libc::getpeereid(stream.as_raw_fd(), &mut uid, &mut gid) } != 0 {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        uid
    };
    #[cfg(not(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")))]
    return Err(io::Error::other(
        "release control peer credentials unsupported on this Unix host",
    ));
    #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
    if uid != unsafe { libc::geteuid() } {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control requires the same filesystem owner",
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn socket_entry(path: &Path) -> io::Result<fs::Metadata> {
    let m = fs::symlink_metadata(path)?;
    if !m.file_type().is_socket()
        || m.uid() != unsafe { libc::geteuid() }
        || m.permissions().mode() & 0o7777 != 0o600
    {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control must be a same-owner 0600 socket, never a symlink or regular file",
        ));
# LLP 3000.000.000.001: Staging with real users

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 deployment, password identity, development doors, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (STAGE)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-18
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Proof kit](./3000.011.003.000.000-packaged-proof-kit.rfc.md), [Store ownership](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md#34-p8-residency-you-can-see-and-a-cli-that-does-not-fight-the-dev-server)

## 1. Commission and boundary

Implementer: STAGE, 2026-10-18, commissioned by Charlie's wave-16 bakeoff
fixes #1/#4. Ten Muse probes used dev because password-role provisioning
had no single recipe. This amendment supplies that recipe using the existing
signup and service-seat authority. No system seed door is added to serve.
SEC owns self-promotion lints and security invariants; KIT4 owns unrelated
proof helpers, PROD2 owns production starter adaptation.

`deploy <dir> --staging` classifies a **new deployment store**, under its
exclusive owner, with durable metadata `deployment_environment=staging`.
An existing store with no classification is production, including legacy stores;
it cannot be relabelled. A staging store remains staging through deploy,
rollback, restart, copying and source edits. There is no promote/relabel flag:
production requires a separate fresh deployment without --staging and real
operator provisioning. --check never creates the classification. Repeating
--staging on an already staging store is allowed, including while serve runs;
it verifies the existing classification without changing it. Malicious edits by
the filesystem/database owner are outside the threat model, as for service tokens.
This is a deployment-lifetime boundary stronger than a mutable per-release flag:
production source or a seed plan cannot opt a production store into test setup.

## 2. Real staging accounts

`GET /auth/config` reports `development` independently of door availability,
`devDoors`, and `environment` (`development`, `staging`, or `production`).
Missing/unknown environment metadata is production. serve remains bearer-only
in both environments, with the same password verifiers, session lifetimes,
rules, rate limits, clock and data plane. No password hashing or grant bypass.

`startServe(dir, options)` additionally returns `operatorToken(service =
"operator", days = 1)` and `seedAccounts(options)`. Minting uses the existing
local CLI proof protocol, captures secret stdout in memory and never writes
tokens into proof logs. It confers only that service seat's existing authority.
Tokens may also be requested on production for first-admin setup.

`seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` creates N real password accounts per
role, then calls the declared mutation as service:operator; `args(account)`
maps the account to application arguments (default `{principal, role}`).
`provision` is a generated mutation handle or `{name}`, including qualified names such as `accounts.provision`. The returned records
contain email, password, principal, token and expiresAt, grouped by role.
`startServe(dir, {seedUsers: options})` performs the same setup before returning
and exposes those records as `sessions`. Before minting or creating accounts,
the helper requires environment=staging and development=false. Production,
legacy and dev hosts refuse. Inputs are validated before effects; signup is
sequential, bounded, respects identity throttling, and never retries an ambiguous
write. A fresh call creates a fresh random email/password set. Partial failure
does not claim rollback: some accounts/grants may exist; discard the disposable
staging deployment or inspect it before retrying. Application data is created
through ordinary authenticated mutations after provisioning.

Credential records are returned to the operator, never logged by the helper.
The tested guide writes them to an exclusively created 0600 file. They must
not enter source control or reports. There is no public provisioning HTTP
endpoint beyond the application's declared rule-checked mutation.

## 3. First admin

The tested guide uses password signup (which grants no role), then the
filesystem owner's `operatorToken()` to invoke an operator-only mutation.
The roster's insert rule independently requires service:operator. No "first
caller wins", empty-roster bypass, client-selected signup role, or permanent
operator credential in the frontend. The same recipe works on production
with explicit operator-selected email/password; bulk seedAccounts refuses there.
Privileged account tokens cannot mint service tokens. SEC owns lints for
self-bootstrapped global authority; the guide points to this ownership.

## 4. Development doors

For password-only schemas (`auth.password && !auth.guests`), dev disables
`/seed`, every `/dev/*` door (except the existing cryptographically protected
local token protocol), and persona headers by default. The pre-existing serve
GET /dev/latency inspection remains service-authenticated; it never admits an
unauthenticated caller and its reset remains refused. `dev
--unsafe-dev-doors` is explicit opt-in with a prominent warning; serve accepts
no such flag. Host/Origin loopback fences still apply. Door availability is
computed from the current adopted schema, so hot adoption to password-only
also closes them. Seed/reset, clock advance and dev job enqueue revalidate
against the engine schema under the writer immediately before privileged effects;
slow request bodies and requests queued behind adoption cannot retain authority.
Action admission/replay, session-route admission, persona cancellation and upload
owner state transitions likewise revalidate under their actual writer. Previously
admitted durable work retains the existing work-record contract; a refused
new request creates no claimable work.
Persona reads also revalidate against the engine used to evaluate the query,
sync page or asset grant, checking the current declared cast at that read’s
admission. In-flight reads retain their existing snapshot semantics: cast
publication does not lock out concurrent ordinary writes, and the engine
transaction rejects a replaced execution schema.
Change-poll presence entry revalidates under its engine. A detached old
schema is fenced by the engine transaction; a replacement password-only reader
cannot inherit a pre-adoption persona capability. A persona header must name an explicitly declared persona;
an empty declaration admits none. Persona-based schemas retain their ordinary
development workflow. /auth/config always reports development=true on dev,
even with closed doors, so probe tooling can refuse it.

`startDev` follows native persistent storage by default, consuming the old
--no-memory alias; --memory/--fresh explicitly request disposable storage.
The returned now() refuses with guidance if the clock door is closed. Native
readiness plus the per-launch proof marker still establish host identity when
/dev/status is unavailable. Existing fixture/clock tests explicitly opt into
unsafe dev doors when their password schema requires those tools. Generated
frontend proofs explicitly select --memory and --unsafe-dev-doors, preserving
disposable fixture isolation and repeatability under the persistent helper
default. Exact 0.4.30/0.4.31 proof templates are retained and safely adapted
by digest; custom scripts remain untouched. DAY5 owns other starter custody.

## 5. Evidence

Required: production relabelling refusal before writes; staging retention over
hot/cold release switches; real signup/login per role and operator-only grants;
self-grant and unauthenticated doors refused; default/explicit dev doors and
schema-adoption closure; persistent/default and disposable dev; private token
logs; the exact guide recipe executed from the packed tarball in Linux
qualification, including production bulk-seed refusal and first admin; three
copied wave-16 dev probes converted to serve. Full crate tests, affected client
tests, Darwin affected tests on the assigned Mac Studio, ref-check, docs budget
and root inventory/guide checks. Never use the full scripts/test.sh here;
qualify-linux --consumer-only complements the manually run crate suites.

## 6. Review record

authority through password-only adoption; a test initializer missed the new
field; qualified mutation handles refused; affected old fixtures needed explicit
unsafe-door opt-in. The writer now rechecks all privileged effects, with
deterministic slow-body/queued-request tests. The initializer, qualified-module
regression, diagnostic assertions and password witness fixture are corrected.
Verbatim review and provenance: [Codex r1](./reviews/3000.000.000.001-stage-r1.codex.txt).
Fresh Codex round 2 was NOT READY on a remaining read gap between persona
authentication and reader acquisition. Reader/locked-engine persona revalidation
and a deterministic memory/file-backed replacement-reader barrier address it.
Muse round 3 returned READY on f5c19ff after provider retries; that earlier
revision is not final approval of subsequent fixes. Codex round 3 found an
exclusive-cast-lock deadlock and round 4 found action/session-route admission
missing writer revalidation. Read admission now samples cast without holding
its publication mutex through I/O; the engine still fences execution-schema
replacement. Actions, cancellations, session routes and upload-owner transitions
recheck under the writer, with durable-work and intent-barrier regressions.
Round 5 independently found stale published fixture examples and an obsolete

    // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.001#4-declaration-and-recipe-corrections — preserve value compatibility without erasing precise assignment domains.
    pub(crate) fn value_compatible(&self, other: &Ty) -> bool {
        let a = self.required();
        let b = other.required();
        match (&a, &b) {
            (Ty::Text(_) | Ty::Enum(_), Ty::Text(_) | Ty::Enum(_)) => true,
            (a, b) if a.sequence().is_some() && b.sequence().is_some() => {
                let (a, _) = a.sequence().unwrap();
                let (b, _) = b.sequence().unwrap();
                a.value_compatible(&b)

            }

        }

        }

    /// The type both branches can share. Row-kind conflicts are errors at
    /// their first join, including inside objects and collections.
    pub fn join(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {

    }

    /// Nonempty list items must never erase a conflict into Unknown: a later

    pub(crate) fn join_item(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {

    fn join_inner(&self, other: &Ty, strict: bool) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        if self == other { return Ok(self.clone()); }
        // Absence adds nullability; it supplies no competing wildcard kind.
        if *self == Ty::Null { return Ok(other.clone().optional()); }
        if *other == Ty::Null { return Ok(self.clone().optional()); }

            (Ty::Unknown, other) | (other, Ty::Unknown) if !strict && !other.contains_row() => other.clone(),
