//! Compatible native device ownership, retained by the SQLite store.
use super::*;

impl Store {
    // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — native entry points share the sidecar flock, leaving SQLite's inode locks alone.
    pub fn open_shared(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            use std::{fs, os::unix::fs::MetadataExt, sync::Arc};
            let failure = |e| RuntimeError::Store(format!("device store at {}: {e}; reopen a regular device store", path.display()));
            for _ in 0..16 {
                let real = match path.canonicalize() {
                    Ok(real) => real,
                    Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
                        let parent = path.parent().filter(|p| !p.as_os_str().is_empty()).unwrap_or(Path::new("."));
                        crate::private_fs::directory(parent).map_err(&failure)?;
                        parent.canonicalize().map_err(&failure)?.join(path.file_name().ok_or_else(|| failure(std::io::Error::other("missing filename")))?)
                    }
                    Err(e) => return Err(failure(e)),
                };
                let before = crate::private_fs::database_metadata(&real).map_err(&failure)?;
                let owner = crate::private_fs::owner_file(&real).map_err(&failure)?;
                owner.try_lock_shared().map_err(|e| match e {
                    fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock => RuntimeError::StoreBusy { path: real.display().to_string() },
                    fs::TryLockError::Error(e) => failure(e),
                })?;
                if !crate::private_fs::owner_is_named(&real, &owner) { continue; }
                // Create only after ownership; WouldBlock leaves a moved
                // store's old path absent instead of manufacturing a database.
                crate::private_fs::database_file(&real).map_err(&failure)?;
                let meta = crate::private_fs::database_metadata(&real).map_err(&failure)?
                    .ok_or_else(|| failure(std::io::Error::other("database disappeared during ownership acquisition")))?;
                let identity = (meta.dev(), meta.ino());
                if before.is_some_and(|m| (m.dev(), m.ino()) != identity) { continue; }
                let mut store = Self::open_base(&real, std::time::Duration::from_secs(5), false)?;
                if store.file_identity != Some(identity) {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("owned database at {} moved or was replaced; stop and reopen its current path", real.display())));
                }
                if !crate::private_fs::owner_is_named(&real, &owner) {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("ownership sidecar at {} changed during open; stop all owners and reopen the store; leave <database>-owner in place because deleting it while a process runs ends exclusion", real.display())));
                }
                crate::private_fs::database_metadata(&real).map_err(&failure)?;
                store.retain_ownership(Arc::new(owner))?;
                return Ok(store);
            }
            Err(failure(std::io::Error::other("database path kept changing during ownership acquisition")))
        }
        #[cfg(not(unix))]
        Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("device ownership at {} requires Unix", path.display())))
    }
}

impl Store {
    // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — retain the lock for the connection's lifetime; exclusion requires leaving its sidecar in place.
    #[cfg(unix)]
    pub fn retain_ownership(&mut self, owner: std::sync::Arc<std::fs::File>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.ownership = Some(owner);
        self.check_ownership()
    }

    pub fn check_ownership(&self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        #[cfg(unix)]
        if self.ownership.is_some() {
            let path = Path::new(self.conn.path().unwrap_or_default());
            // Preserve the stat-only database/hard-link check. This is not
            // revocation: raw conn() users do not pass through this method.
            // Deleting the sidecar ends exclusion without stopping old work.
            crate::private_fs::database_metadata(path)
                .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(format!("database at {}: {e}", path.display())))?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — ownership flocks belong beside SQLite, never on its database inode.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn owner_path(database: &Path) -> std::path::PathBuf {
    let mut name = database.as_os_str().to_owned();
    name.push("-owner");
    name.into()
}

/// Stat only: opening and closing a hard-linked database loses SQLite locks.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn database_metadata(database: &Path) -> io::Result<Option<fs::Metadata>> {
    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
    match fs::symlink_metadata(database) {
        Ok(meta) if !meta.is_file() => Err(io::Error::other("database must be a regular file")),
        Ok(meta) if meta.nlink() != 1 => Err(io::Error::other("database has hard links; copy the store, do not hard-link it")),
        Ok(meta) => Ok(Some(meta)),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
        Err(e) => Err(e),
    }
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn owner_file(database: &Path) -> io::Result<File> {
    auxiliary_file(database, &owner_path(database), true, 0)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — preflight aliases; an ambiguous descriptor must leak rather than close SQLite's POSIX locks.
/// Ownership files are empty; address records fit in 128 bytes. The size
/// bound also rejects a database renamed here after its original name vanished.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn auxiliary_file(database: &Path, path: &Path, create: bool, max_len: u64) -> io::Result<File> {
    let before = match fs::symlink_metadata(database) {
        Ok(meta) => Some(meta),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => None,
        Err(e) => return Err(e),
    };
    match fs::symlink_metadata(path) {
        Ok(meta) => auxiliary_metadata(&meta, before.as_ref(), max_len)?,
        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {},
        Err(e) => return Err(e),
    }
    let file = OpenOptions::new().read(true).write(create).create(create).truncate(false)
        .mode(0o600).custom_flags(libc::O_NOFOLLOW | libc::O_NONBLOCK).open(path)?;
    let file = checked_auxiliary(file, database, before.as_ref(), max_len)?;
    if create { file.set_permissions(fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600))?; }
    Ok(file)
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn auxiliary_metadata(meta: &fs::Metadata, database: Option<&fs::Metadata>, max_len: u64) -> io::Result<()> {
    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
    if !meta.is_file() || meta.nlink() != 1 || meta.len() > max_len
        || database.is_some_and(|db| (meta.dev(), meta.ino()) == (db.dev(), db.ino())) {
        Err(io::Error::other("ownership/address file must be a separate regular single-linked file of the expected size; stop the owners and restore the store and its sidecars"))
    } else { Ok(()) }
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn checked_auxiliary(file: File, database: &Path, before: Option<&fs::Metadata>, max_len: u64) -> io::Result<File> {
    // Inspect both the pre-open and current database identities. On any
    // uncertainty, do not flock, chmod, read, write, unlock, or close the fd.
    let checked = (|| {
        let meta = file.metadata()?;
        auxiliary_metadata(&meta, before, max_len)?;
        match fs::symlink_metadata(database) {
            Ok(db) => auxiliary_metadata(&meta, Some(&db), max_len),
            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(()),
            Err(e) => Err(e),
        }
    })();
    if let Err(e) = checked {
        std::mem::forget(file);
        return Err(e);
    }
    Ok(file)
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn owner_is_named(database: &Path, file: &File) -> bool {
    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
    fs::symlink_metadata(owner_path(database)).is_ok_and(|named| {
        named.is_file() && named.nlink() == 1 && file.metadata().is_ok_and(|held| {
            (named.dev(), named.ino()) == (held.dev(), held.ino())
        })
    })

        if let Value::Row(inputs)|Value::Map(inputs)=e.inputs {for(c,value)in inputs {
            if e.absent.contains(&c) || !fields.contains_key(&c) || fields.get(&c)==Some(&value) {
                let invalid:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND invalid=1)",params![t,id,input_ref(&key,&c)],|r|r.get(0))?;
                if invalid {return Err(refusal(format!("historical backfill reference {c} on {id} lost its parent lifetime")));}
                if e.absent.contains(&c) {fields.insert(c,value);}else{fields.entry(c).or_insert(value);}
            }
        }}
        for c in e.after.keys() {if !e.before.contains_key(c) {fields.insert(c.clone(),Value::Null);}}
        for(c,value)in e.before {fields.insert(c,value);}
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;
    } Ok(())
}
fn rebuild_reads(tx:&Tx<'_>,source:&Schema,reads:&BTreeSet<(String,String)>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    for (name,index) in reads {
        let old=&source.tables[name].indexes[index];let ti=tx.num(&format!("{name}.{index}"))?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti=?1",[ti])?;
        super::activation::walk(tx,tx.num(name)?,|id,mut row|{
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,&source.tables[name]);
            for key in index_keys(old,&row,id){tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO i(ti,k,id) VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![ti,key,id])?;}Ok(())
        })?;
        if old.unique {validate_lookup(tx,ti,old.components.len(),name,index)?;}
    } Ok(())
}

fn input_ref(key:&str,column:&str)->String {format!("$backfill:{key}:{column}")}

//! Release backfills use the same activation transaction as retention and indexes.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#3-adoption-and-reversibility — stage every result before mutating the input snapshot.
use super::*;
use crate::{schema::{backfill::Backfill, materialize_optional_fields}, engine::{Context, runner::Runner}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
pub(crate) const VERSION: &str = "snapback4-release-store-4";
fn refusal(message: impl Into<String>) -> RuntimeError { RuntimeError::Structural { code:"E_ADOPT", message:message.into() } }
fn present(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_backfill_evidence')",[],|r|r.get(0))?)
}
fn enable(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_evidence(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,inputs TEXT NOT NULL,before_image TEXT NOT NULL,after_image TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,epoch INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_pruned(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_definitions(digest TEXT PRIMARY KEY,definition TEXT NOT NULL);
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_absence(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,column_name TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition,column_name)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_inputs(t INTEGER NOT NULL,column_name TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,column_name)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_active(t INTEGER NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,input_names TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    if !tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM pragma_table_info('_backfill_evidence') WHERE name='epoch')",[],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
        tx.conn().execute_batch("ALTER TABLE _backfill_evidence ADD COLUMN epoch INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;")?;
    }
    if !tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM pragma_table_info('_backfill_active') WHERE name='input_names')",[],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
        tx.conn().execute_batch("ALTER TABLE _backfill_active ADD COLUMN input_names TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]';")?;
    }
    tx.set_meta("version", VERSION)
}
#[derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
struct Evidence { #[serde(default)] absent:BTreeSet<String>, #[serde(default)] epoch:i64, active:bool, inputs: Value, before: BTreeMap<String,Value>, after: BTreeMap<String,Value> }
fn evidence(tx: &Tx<'_>, t:i64,id:&str,key:&str) -> Result<Option<Evidence>,RuntimeError> {
    let bytes:Option<i64>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT octet_length(inputs)+octet_length(before_image)+octet_length(after_image) FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
    let mut admission=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);admission.heap(bytes.unwrap_or(0).max(0) as u64 * 64 + 8192)?;
    let value:Option<(String,String,String,bool,i64)>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT inputs,before_image,after_image,active,epoch FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?,r.get(2)?,r.get(3)?,r.get(4)?))).optional()?;
    value.map(|(inputs,before,after,active,epoch)|Ok(Evidence {absent:absence(tx,t,id,key)?,epoch,active,inputs:decode(&inputs)?,before:decode(&before)?,after:decode(&after)?})).transpose()
}
fn decode<T:serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(s:&str)->Result<T,RuntimeError>{serde_json::from_str(s).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))}
fn absence(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,key:&str)->Result<BTreeSet<String>,RuntimeError>{
    Ok(tx.conn().prepare("SELECT column_name FROM _backfill_absence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3")?.query_map(params![t,id,key],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?)
}
fn save(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,key:&str,e:&Evidence,active:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _backfill_evidence(t,id,definition,inputs,before_image,after_image,active,epoch) VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7,?8) ON CONFLICT(t,id,definition) DO UPDATE SET inputs=excluded.inputs,before_image=excluded.before_image,after_image=excluded.after_image,active=excluded.active,epoch=excluded.epoch",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&e.inputs),canonical_json(&e.before),canonical_json(&e.after),active,e.epoch])?;
    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key])?;
    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_absence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key])?;
    for c in &e.absent {tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _backfill_absence VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4)",params![t,id,key,c])?;}
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn delete(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if present(tx)? {
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_absence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
    } Ok(())
}
// Never archive reference/asset preimages without lifetime evidence. New reference
// fields are safe: subsequent retirement is owned by the ordinary dormant ledger.
fn protected(kind:&crate::schema::ColumnType)->bool {
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match kind {Optional(k)=>protected(k),Ref{..}|Image{..}|Video{..}|File{..}|List{..}|ListRange{..}=>true,_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn transition(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,previous:Option<&Schema>,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,now:i64,report:&mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if next.backfills.is_empty() && previous.is_none_or(|s|s.backfills.is_empty()) && !present(tx)? { return Ok(()); }
    enable(tx)?;
    for fill in &next.backfills { fill.admit(next).map_err(refusal)?; }
    // A name is immutable within a continuous lineage. Replacing a transform
    // deliberately goes through an old-source/bridge transition first.
    if let Some(old)=previous {for f in &next.backfills {if old.backfills.iter().any(|p|p.table==f.table && p.name==f.name && p.key()!=f.key()) {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} changed; use a new name in a bridge release rather than replacing historical meaning",f.table,f.name)));}}}
    for fill in &next.backfills {
        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_definitions VALUES(?1,?2)",params![fill.key(),canonical_json(fill)])?;
    }
    let next_keys:BTreeSet<_>=next.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key).collect();
    let definitions:Vec<Backfill>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT definition FROM _backfill_definitions WHERE digest IN (SELECT definition FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE active=1 UNION SELECT definition FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE active=1) ORDER BY digest")?.query_map([],|r|r.get::<_,String>(0))?.map(|s|decode(&s?)).collect::<Result<_,RuntimeError>>()?;
    for fill in definitions.iter().rev().filter(|f|!next_keys.contains(&f.key())) {
        let Some(table)=next.table(&fill.table) else {continue};
        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.key();let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;
        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,mut row|{
            let Some(e)=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)? else {
                let pruned:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3 AND active=1)",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get(0))?;
                if pruned && fill.outputs.iter().any(|c|table.columns.contains_key(c)) {bad.add(id,"rollback preimage was explicitly pruned; select a bridge preserving current data or restore a backup");}
                return Ok(())
            };
            if !e.active {return Ok(())}
            let fields=match &mut row {Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=>f,_=>return Err(refusal("invalid stored row"))};
            let mut changed=false;
            for c in &fill.outputs {if table.columns.contains_key(c) {
                let current=fields.get(c);let before=e.before.get(c);let after=e.after.get(c);
                if current!=after && current!=before {bad.add(id,&format!("{}.{} changed after backfill {}; keep a bridge and repair through an authorized mutation before rollback",fill.table,c,fill.name));return Ok(())}
                if let Some(value)=before {fields.insert(c.clone(),value.clone());}else{fields.remove(c);}
                changed=true;
            }}
            if changed {tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;count+=1;}
            save(tx,t,id,&key,&e,false)
        })?;
        if bad.count>0 {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} rollback: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}
        if count>0 {report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} reversed on {count} {}",fill.table,fill.name,if count==1 {"row"}else{"rows"}));tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;}
    }
    let old_keys:BTreeSet<_>=previous.into_iter().flat_map(|s|s.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key)).collect();
    let restored_tables:BTreeSet<i64>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT n.t FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=n.t AND r.id=n.id)")?.query_map([],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

//! The program IR: what the Quarry binder emits and the interpreter runs, on
//! the server and on a device alike. Copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 2000.000
//! §5.1) in its serialized form, so the conformance corpus loads unchanged;
//! the held-search descriptor, a receipt-era form, is dropped.

pub mod effects;

use crate::value::Value;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

fn is_zero(value: &u32) -> bool {
    *value == 0
}

fn is_false(value: &bool) -> bool {
    !*value
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Program {
    pub name: String,
    pub kind: ProgramKind,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub args: BTreeMap<String, ArgType>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub read_time_granularity_ms: Option<u64>,
    pub body: Vec<Stmt>,
    /// Binder-marked top-level returned shape loop paths and their source read sites.
    /// Empty for all other programs, preserving their serialized form.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "BTreeMap::is_empty")]
    pub returned_shapes: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    /// A job whose body is the app's own wasm module, run by the engine's
    /// native host one chunk per commit (LLP 3000.005). The body is empty.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
    pub native: bool,
}

    Mutation,

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn insert(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, false).map(|_| ())
    }

    pub(super) fn insert_if_absent(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, true)
    }

    fn insert_mode(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        if self.device && if_absent && self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("insert if absent on a read-through child queues without a row".into()));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            let mark = runner.meter.mark();
            let result = runner.insert_attempt(table_name, row, if_absent, None).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, Effect::Insert));
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — denied direct allocations hide deferred
            // read witnesses and counter work just as NOT_FOUND hides targets.
            if matches!(&result, Err(RuntimeError::Rule { effect: Effect::Insert, .. } | RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. }))
                && runner.schema.tables.get(table_name).is_some_and(|table| !table.sequences.is_empty()) {
                runner.meter.restore(mark);
            }
            result
        })
    }

    fn insert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool, upsert_index: Option<&str>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Insert)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, true)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        let supplied_sequence = super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row);
        // A directly supplied field is a payload error before any lookup;
        // only an upsert's copied address waits for insert authority.
        if upsert_index.is_none() && supplied_sequence.is_err() { return supplied_sequence.map(|_| false); }
        let supplied = row.clone();
        let row = self.with_defaults(table_name, row)?;
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].ephemeral.is_some() { self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?; }
        let row = row.ephemeral(&self.schema.tables[table_name], self.context.now.unwrap_or(0))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve, then stamp the parent capture before any rule sees the image.
        self.reserve_capture(&self.schema.tables[table_name])?;
        let row = self.capture_insert(table_name, row)?;
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        let independent = !table.sequences.is_empty() && !super::rules::deciding_fields(table_name, table, &table.rules.insert)
            .iter().any(|field| table.sequences.contains_key(field));
        // An independent denial must never consult a hidden read witness.
        let authorized = independent && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)?;
        if independent && !authorized {
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
        }
        if !table.sequences.is_empty() {
            // Admission reserves the worst case before either read verdict;
            // restoring a refusal alone cannot hide a boundary abort.
            // An independent insert rule was already prepaid and evaluated.
            // Only still-pending checks belong in this preflight allowance.
            let insert_checks = if authorized { 0 } else { 1 + u64::from(upsert_index.is_some() && supplied_sequence.is_ok()) };
            self.reserve_sequence_authorization(table, &row, insert_checks)?;
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — an unreadable allocation cannot expose counter,
        // uniqueness or cap state, even to a principal allowed to insert.
        if !self.sequence_insert_readable(table_name, &row)? {
            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "sequence read authority");
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "sequence read authority") });
        }
        if let Err(error) = supplied_sequence {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — absent upserts decide insert authority before field diagnostics.
            // A target key may supply an ordinal even when the patch does not.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
                return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });

## Authoring and proof refusals
N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER means no production insert path can bootstrap this table. It groups root blockers with their dependents; persona-only schemas get one identity note. Add a service-seat insert mutation and provision real account relationships. E_UPGRADE means installation or post-upgrade check failed. Repair the package-manager or registry error and rerun upgrade. Packages may already be pinned: if check failed, repair the reported schema/device refusal and rerun check.

## Capture a saved version
After check and business cases pass, from the app root:
```sh
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --save-version versions/v1
snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check --bridge ./bridge
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --schema ./bridge --check
```

The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. Unchanged deploy keeps its id; existing snapshots never change. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge starts from complete current Quarry source and this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md`. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.

Replacing an earlier transform first restores its reversible preimages; declare historical input types covering those originals and any native rows from the newer release.

Rows created under a backfilled release keep their authored outputs when the same declaration returns with unchanged inputs. If older code changes those inputs during rollback, re-entry refuses rather than overwriting the authored values; choose an explicit bridge or a new named transform to resolve the intended result.

## Persistent production: v1 → v2 → rollback → forward

Save the following as `day-two-production.mjs` in an app with `snapback4`
installed and run `node day-two-production.mjs`. `PRODUCTION_DIR` selects its
persistent deployment (default `./production-day-two`). Run it again against
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => disallowed_control(inner, Some(value)),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => values.iter().find_map(|value| disallowed_control(of, Some(value))),
        (ColumnType::Text { .. }, Some(Value::String(text))) => disallowed_text_control(text),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Text forbids C0 controls except tab, LF and CR.
pub fn disallowed_text_control(text: &str) -> Option<u32> {
    text.chars().find(|c| c.is_control() && !matches!(c, '\t' | '\n' | '\r')).map(|c| c as u32)
}

pub(crate) fn fits(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> bool {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(_), None | Some(Value::Null)) => true,
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => fits(inner, Some(value)),
        (ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => values.len() <= *max as usize && values.iter().all(|value| fits(of, Some(value))),
        // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — typed references do not imply target existence
        (ColumnType::Id | ColumnType::Principal | ColumnType::Ref { .. }, Some(Value::Id(_))) => true,
        (ColumnType::Text { max }, Some(Value::String(text))) => text.chars().count() <= *max as usize,
        (ColumnType::Bytes { max }, Some(Value::Bytes(bytes))) => bytes.len() <= *max as usize,
        (ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. }, Some(Value::Map(fields))) => fields.get("id").is_some_and(|id| matches!(id, Value::Id(_))),
        (ColumnType::Enum { variants }, Some(Value::String(value))) => variants.contains(value),
        (ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::Time | ColumnType::Decimal { .. } | ColumnType::Money { .. }, Some(Value::Int(value))) => (I53_MIN..=I53_MAX).contains(value),
        // @ref LLP 3000.040#14-where-the-bound-is-enforced — the slot's bound is part of its type: every stored write and argument meets it here.
        (ColumnType::IntRange { min, max }, Some(Value::Int(value))) => (I53_MIN..=I53_MAX).contains(value) && (*min..=*max).contains(value),
        (ColumnType::Bool, Some(Value::Bool(_))) => true,
        (ColumnType::Json { max_bytes }, Some(value)) => canonical_json(value).len() <= *max_bytes as usize,
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Validate the arguments and fill each omitted optional with `null`, so
/// a caller that leaves `c` out reads page one.
#[cfg(not(all(target_arch = "wasm32", feature = "web", not(feature = "sqlite"))))]
pub fn validate_inputs(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    super::admission::check(schema, program)?;
    validate_arguments(schema, program, inputs)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.002#proposed-device-representation-and-codec-redesign — the web predicts a server-admitted backend; the server and native hosts still check every call.
pub(super) fn validate_device_inputs(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    #[cfg(not(any(all(target_arch = "wasm32", feature = "web", not(feature = "sqlite")), feature = "web-device-check")))]
    super::admission::check(schema, program)?;
    validate_arguments(schema, program, inputs)
}

fn validate_arguments(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (name, kind) in &program.args {
        if !inputs.contains_key(name) && (kind.is_optional() || matches!(kind, ArgType::Cursor)) {
            inputs.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
        }
        let value = inputs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input(format!("missing argument {name}")))?;
        validate_arg(schema, kind, value, name)?;
    }

# LLP 3000.000.000.002: Declarative release backfills

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language, storage, release adoption, staging clock, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (MIG)
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Revised:** 2026-10-19
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Staging](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md)

## 1. Commission

Implementer: MIG, 2026-10-19. Wave 17's fifty changes require constant,
conditional, derived, enum, string-split and point-lookup transformations.
Declare these in Quarry, with no public migration mutation, job or JavaScript.
REL5 owns release selection/restoration and old-client compatibility; SEC3 owns
security checks. No new version, publication or push authority is implied.

## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Continuous carry-forward keeps the
same normalized definition; changing it under the same name refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
   expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-4`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.

Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Patches remain staged until
all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
New/inactive declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot and
catch up changed old inputs. Declared historical arguments consume retained
cells and source definitions even when the candidate omits those columns;
retained parent-lifetime bindings still apply. A native-image exception requires a birth marker for this exact declaration,
a matching fingerprint of the original non-output inputs, and valid, complete outputs;
a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
transformation left a retained output. Receipt reference inputs use private namespaced bindings in the existing
dormant parent-lifetime ledger, independently of the live input column. A
retargeted/dropped input cannot substitute its current binding for the saved
reference. Reconstruction validates any consumed saved binding before restoring
its value. Original absence is represented as explicit null in the historical
interpretation, preventing later defaults from filling it during argument or
lookup/index evaluation. Expired sources retire when entering or leaving the
last declaration, including restored table images.

When transformed rows become dormant, candidate output reference types supply
parent bindings even when the predecessor had no such column. Existing invalid
bindings are preserved. If a restored row becomes dormant again, only its
explicitly backfilled authoritative cells update the retained image; original
source definition, other cells and authority provenance stay intact. Forward
selection after rollback reconstructs absent added outputs for that same
declaration before evaluating its arguments, rather than feeding it its own
retained result.

Input omission is stored independently from interpreted null/default values,
both for the current physical lifetime and for each receipt. Ordinary writes
compare interpreted old/new inputs; meaningful edits end omission provenance,
while unrelated writes and default materialization do not. Receipts reconstruct
their original interpretation of omitted fields across later default changes.

Rows inserted while a declaration is active receive a private native-birth
marker keyed by its normalized definition and a hash of their input image;
no original input values are kept in that marker. The same declaration preserves
complete native images with unchanged inputs, including absent optional inputs,
constant transforms and whole-table restoration. Changed native inputs, invalid
present inputs, lost native output images or conflicting other-transform history
refuse with a bridge reason. An inactive overlapping receipt is compatible only
when every overlapping current output equals its recorded preimage; an active
or pruned conflicting history still refuses. Same-column output values belong
to the candidate type, rather than the historical input type, for native rows.
A new declaration name does not inherit another
name's birth marker. Prune retains only native fingerprints for surviving
lifetimes, alongside current omission flags; receipt absence flags are forgotten
with historical values. Delete/reset remove every marker for the ended lifetime.

Source retirement uses the ordinary engine expiry/reference-action closure
under the complete assembled predecessor schema, before any patch runs or
removed table is archived. Only surviving post-action rows enter dormant storage. Restored source
indexes and cap occupancy are rebuilt first; writing-reference groups must fit
the source reservation's live bound. Cascade and set-null effects participate
in this same transaction; restrict or retained-child blockers refuse the entire
adoption. Retired source population forces a new generation so clients cannot
keep children removed by a cascade. Authoritative set-null clears corresponding
old column bindings, while receipt input bindings remain independent. An expired
narrowed source is exposed only inside this uncommitted lifecycle phase and
cannot reach a backfill evaluation alive.

Development reset still empties every lifetime's data/history/native/omission
markers, then reconstructs code-derived declaration/input registries from its
retained schema before reseeding; the new identity's rows receive new birth
markers. No release HTTP reset door is added.

Evidence decode is preflighted against
the existing job heap ceiling. Text preview lists private preimages separately
from dormant application rows/cells/index entries.

## 4. Staging clock

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification. Each helper control
mints a current staging-time operator session rather than caching a credential
across arbitrary time jumps. Ordinary user sessions retain normal expiry.

Hot release activation samples `shared.now()` under its engine writer, including
the persisted disclosure floor, and passes that one time through the core's
explicit `adopt_release_at`. A live preview probes the existing filesystem owner lock independently of
HTTP bind/address hints and acquires a stopped-store lock before taking its
SQLite snapshot, retaining that lock through inspection.
A live preview samples time through the same-owner,
store-bound existing private status protocol; no session is minted or clock
changed. A revision mismatch with the private database snapshot refuses as a
racing activation. Store contents and the clock are point-in-time samples, not
a freeze of later live writes. A stopped store's cold activation/preview uses
wall time, with the existing persisted disclosure floor applied by activation.
Positive and negative staging offsets are both covered by hot/preview tests.

## 5. Evidence

Tests cover all fifty historical shapes, no-op/restart, v1 writes during

//! Privileged, bounded population evidence for adoption refusals.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#3-older-writes-and-incompatible-values — count all affected rows, retain at most five samples.
use super::*;
use crate::schema::{Constraint, materialize_optional_fields};

#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Samples {
    pub count: u64,
    ids: Vec<String>,
    values: Vec<String>,
    last_id: Option<String>,
}
pub(super) fn bounded(text: &str) -> String {
    if text.len() <= 256 {
        return text.into();
    }
    let mut end = 256;
    while !text.is_char_boundary(end) {
        end -= 1;
    }
    format!("{}… [truncated]", &text[..end])
}

fn value_sample(value: &Value) -> String {
    struct Prefix(Vec<u8>);
    impl std::io::Write for Prefix {
        fn write(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<usize> {
            let n = bytes.len().min(256 - self.0.len());
            self.0.extend_from_slice(&bytes[..n]);
            if n < bytes.len() {
                return Err(std::io::Error::other("sample truncated"));
            }
            Ok(n)
        }
        fn flush(&mut self) -> std::io::Result<()> {
            Ok(())
        }
    }
    if let Value::Bytes(bytes) = value {
        return format!("{} bytes", bytes.len());
    }
    let mut prefix = Prefix(Vec::with_capacity(256));
    let truncated = serde_json::to_writer(&mut prefix, &crate::wire::Plain(value)).is_err();
    let mut text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&prefix.0).into_owned();
    if truncated {
        text.push_str("… [truncated]")
    }
    text
}
impl Samples {
    pub fn add(&mut self, id: &str, value: &str) {
        if self.last_id.as_deref() != Some(id) {
            self.count += 1;
            if self.ids.len() < 5 {
                self.ids.push(bounded(id));
            }
            self.last_id = Some(id.into());
        }
        let value = bounded(value);
        if self.values.len() < 5 && !self.values.contains(&value) {
            self.values.push(value);
        }
    }
    pub fn describe(&self) -> String {
        format!(
            "{} affected rows; sample ids [{}]; values/keys [{}]",
            self.count,
            self.ids.join(", "),
            self.values.join(", ")
        )
    }
}

pub(super) fn candidate(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    schema: &Schema,
    previous: Option<&Schema>,
    complete: bool,
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (name, table) in schema
        .tables
        .iter()
        .filter(|(n, t)| t.ephemeral.is_none() && schema.maintain_for(n).is_none())
    {
        if previous.and_then(|p| p.table(name)) == Some(table) {
            continue;
        }
        let mut bad = Samples::default();
        super::activation::walk(tx, tx.num(name)?, |id, mut row| {
            if let Value::Row(f) | Value::Map(f) = &mut row {
                f.retain(|c, _| table.columns.contains_key(c));
            }
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            let validation = if complete {
                crate::engine::validate::validate_row(schema, name, &row)
            } else {
                crate::engine::validate::validate_adoption_input(schema, name, &row)
            };
            if let Err(error) = validation {
                let mut detailed = false;
                for (column, kind) in &table.columns {
                    if (complete || !table.engine_field(column))
                        && crate::engine::validate::validate_column(
                            name,
                            column,
                            kind,
                            row.field(column).ok(),
                        )
                        .is_err()
                    {
                        let value = row
                            .field(column)
                            .map(value_sample)
                            .unwrap_or_else(|_| "<missing>".into());
                        bad.add(id, &format!("{name}.{column}={value}; {error}"));

    pub fn assignable_to(&self, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
        if self == expected {
            return true;
        }
        match (self, expected) {
            (Ty::Null, Ty::Optional(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Optional(inner), Ty::Optional(target)) => inner.assignable_to(target),
            (value, Ty::Optional(target)) => value.assignable_to(target),
            (Ty::Text(a), Ty::Text(b)) => a <= b,
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20 — enum widening is static and total: every variant fits the text bound and character domain.
            (Ty::Enum(variants), Ty::Text(max)) => variants.iter().all(|v| v.chars().count() <= *max as usize && snapback4_core::engine::validate::disallowed_text_control(v).is_none()),
            // A text literal in an enum position is a variant; the binder
            // checks membership where it knows the literal.
            (Ty::Text(_), Ty::Enum(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Id, Ty::Ref(_)) | (Ty::Ref(_), Ty::Id) | (Ty::Id, Ty::Principal) | (Ty::Principal, Ty::Id) => true,
            (Ty::Ref(a), Ty::Ref(b)) => a == b,
            (Ty::Int, Ty::Time) | (Ty::Time, Ty::Int) => true,
            (Ty::Int, Ty::Decimal(_)) | (Ty::Int, Ty::Money(..)) => true,

**NOT READY**

1. **[P1] Generic `id` arguments bypass saved reference lifetime protection.** [backfills.rs:226](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:226) captures receipt bindings using the declaration’s argument type. A historical reference declared as `parent: id` passes validation but creates no parent binding. Apply a lookup backfill using parent P, retarget the live reference to Q, drop that column, delete/reuse P, then remove/restore the child table. Reconstruction can restore saved P and read its replacement lifetime without refusing. Capture bindings from historical source reference types, or reject declarations that erase that relationship. Add this regression alongside the existing typed-reference test.

2. **[P1] Physically omitted defaulted inputs become null.** [backfills.rs:169](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:169) resolves inputs before source interpretation, and [native_inputs:409](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:409) maps omission directly to null. Create a row before `n` exists, add `n: int default 3`, then introduce `{doubled: n * 2}`. Default adoption leaves `n` physically absent, so the backfill refuses required `n`; an optional argument silently computes from null instead of 3. Preserve physical omission separately from the predecessor’s interpreted value, and retain that original interpretation through later default changes.
